11. Core Schema and Core Types

11.1 pcs-core — Task, TaskId, TaskState

TaskId

#[derive(Copy, Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, PartialOrd, Ord)]
pub struct TaskId(pub u64);
Property Detail
Ownership Copy; passed by value everywhere; no lifetime
Mutation immutable after construction; generated by atomic counter in pcs-pool
Concurrency read from any thread without synchronization (plain u64 copy)
Persistence serializable as u64; stable within a single pool lifetime; not stable across restarts
Compatibility additive: new fields prohibited; TaskId(0) reserved as sentinel/null

TaskState

#[derive(Copy, Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
#[repr(u8)]
pub enum TaskState {
    Pending   = 0,  // allocated, not yet enqueued
    Ready     = 1,  // in a deque or injector; not running
    Running   = 2,  // executing on exactly one worker
    Done      = 3,  // FnOnce completed normally
    Panicked  = 4,  // FnOnce panicked; payload held by scope
}

State transition table:

Pending → Ready     : enqueue to deque / injector
Ready   → Running   : worker pops or steals (ownership transfer; I1)
Running → Done      : FnOnce returns normally
Running → Panicked  : FnOnce unwinds; catch_unwind in worker loop (I7)
  • AtomicTaskState wraps AtomicU8; transitions use compare_exchange to enforce the DAG.
  • No backwards transitions; terminal states Done/Panicked are absorbing.

Task

pub struct Task {
    pub id:    TaskId,
    pub state: AtomicTaskState,
    run:       UnsafeCell<Option<Box<dyn FnOnce() + Send + 'static>>>,
}
Property Detail
Ownership heap-allocated; owned by the deque slot or Injector node until stolen/run
Mutation run field consumed once (Option → None after execution); state mutated via atomics
Concurrency Send (closure is Send); UnsafeCell for run protected by TaskState ownership protocol (I1)
Persistence not serializable; lifetime bounded by pool session
Compatibility run field private; id and state stable; extra fields allowed behind feature flags

AtomicTaskState

pub struct AtomicTaskState(AtomicU8);
impl AtomicTaskState {
    pub fn load(&self, ord: Ordering) -> TaskState;
    pub fn transition(&self, from: TaskState, to: TaskState) -> Result<(), TaskState>;
}
  • transition is a single compare_exchange; returns Err(actual) on failure.
  • Callers must re-check actual state and handle contention.

RawJob

pub(crate) struct RawJob {
    fn_ptr: unsafe fn(*mut ()),
    data:   *mut (),
}
unsafe impl Send for RawJob {}
  • Type-erased trampoline for zero-alloc task bodies on stack-pinned scopes.
  • Used by pcs-parallel scope internals; not part of the public API.

11.2 pcs-deque — ChaseLevDeque, Stealer, CircularBuffer

ChaseLevDeque<T>

pub struct ChaseLevDeque<T> {
    bottom: AtomicIsize,
    top:    AtomicIsize,
    buffer: Atomic<Arc<CircularBuffer<T>>>,
}
Method Ordering contract
push(&self, val: T) Release on bottom; wait-free for owner
pop(&self) -> Steal<T> Acquire on top + bottom; wait-free unless concurrent steal
stealer(&self) -> Stealer<T> returns cloneable handle; zero-cost
  • Owner thread: only caller of push / pop.
  • Thief threads: only callers of Stealer::steal.
  • Buffer grows (double) when bottom - top >= capacity; old buffer kept alive until all thieves drain.
  • Monotonic top increment (epoch) defeats ABA (I3); referenced in ADR 0001.

Steal<T>

pub enum Steal<T> {
    Success(T),  // item obtained
    Empty,       // deque was empty
    Retry,       // CAS contention; caller should back-off and retry
}

Stealer<T>

pub struct Stealer<T> {
    deque: Arc<ChaseLevDeque<T>>,
}
impl<T: Send> Clone for Stealer<T> { ... }
impl<T: Send> Stealer<T> {
    pub fn steal(&self) -> Steal<T>;
}
Property Detail
Ownership Arc-shared reference to owner's deque; Clone is cheap
Mutation steal performs CAS on top; at most one thief wins per item (I2)
Concurrency Send + Sync; multiple thieves race on top CAS
Persistence not persistent; valid only while owning Worker is alive

CircularBuffer<T>

pub(crate) struct CircularBuffer<T> {
    capacity: usize,      // power of 2
    mask:     usize,      // capacity - 1
    slots:    Box<[UnsafeCell<MaybeUninit<T>>]>,
}
impl<T> CircularBuffer<T> {
    pub fn new(cap: usize) -> Self;           // cap must be power of 2
    pub unsafe fn read(&self, idx: isize) -> T;
    pub unsafe fn write(&self, idx: isize, val: T);
    pub fn grow(&self, bottom: isize, top: isize) -> Arc<Self>;
}
  • read/write are unsafe; callers ensure exclusive ownership via deque protocol.
  • grow copies live elements to a 2× buffer; old Arc dropped after all steal CAS paths.

11.3 pcs-worker — Worker, WorkerHandle

Worker

pub struct Worker {
    pub id:       usize,
    pub local:    ChaseLevDeque<Task>,
    pub stealers: Box<[Stealer<Task>]>,
    pub rng:      XorShift,
    pub numa:     NumaNode,
    pub stats:    Arc<SchedMetrics>,
}
Property Detail
Ownership one Worker per OS thread; local deque is thread-private
Mutation local mutated by owner only; stealers slice is read-only after construction
Concurrency Worker itself is !Sync (owner thread only); referenced via WorkerHandle cross-thread
rng XorShift (no Send/Sync); used only in owner thread for victim selection 
impl Worker {
    pub fn run_local(&mut self) -> bool;           // pop + execute one task; return true if ran
    pub fn steal_from_victim(&mut self) -> bool;   // attempt steal from RNG-selected victim
    pub fn park_until_notified(&mut self);         // exponential backoff → OS park
}

WorkerHandle

pub struct WorkerHandle {
    unpark: Arc<AtomicBool>,
    thread: Thread,                               // std::thread::Thread for unpark()
}
impl WorkerHandle {
    pub fn notify(&self);                          // set flag + unpark thread
}
  • Shared between pool and workers for unpark signaling.
  • Arc<AtomicBool> flag prevents spurious re-parks.

XorShift

pub struct XorShift { state: u64 }
impl XorShift {
    pub fn new(seed: u64) -> Self;
    pub fn next_usize(&mut self, bound: usize) -> usize;  // uniform in [0, bound)
}
  • No Send; single-threaded use only; seeded from worker.id XOR pool start-time.

11.4 pcs-pool — ThreadPool, PoolConfig, Injector, CpuSet

PoolConfig

pub struct PoolConfig {
    pub num_threads:   usize,          // default: logical_cpus()
    pub stack_size:    usize,          // default: 2 MiB
    pub affinity:      Option<CpuSet>, // None = OS default
    pub policy:        SchedPolicy,    // default: WorkFirst
    pub max_in_flight: usize,          // backpressure cap (I6, P10)
}
impl Default for PoolConfig { ... }

CpuSet

pub struct CpuSet {
    bits: Vec<u64>,   // one bit per logical CPU; variable length
}
impl CpuSet {
    pub fn from_cpus(cpus: &[usize]) -> Self;
    pub fn all() -> Self;
    pub fn numa_node(node: NumaNode) -> Self;
    pub fn apply_to_current_thread(&self) -> Result<(), AffinityError>;
}

ThreadPool

pub struct ThreadPool {
    workers:  Vec<WorkerHandle>,
    injector: Arc<Injector<Task>>,
    topology: Topology,
    config:   PoolConfig,
    state:    Arc<PoolState>,
}
impl ThreadPool {
    pub fn new(config: PoolConfig) -> Self;
    pub fn submit(&self, task: Task) -> Result<(), SubmitError>;   // backpressure: Err if saturated
    pub fn scope<'s, F: FnOnce(Scope<'s>)>(&'s self, f: F);        // blocks until scope done
    pub fn shutdown(self);                                           // drain + join all threads (I10)
}
Property Detail
Ownership owns all Worker OS threads; drop = panic if not shut down
Concurrency Arc-shared fields; submit is Send + Sync
Backpressure max_in_flight counter; submit returns Err(SubmitError::Saturated) (P10, I6)
Shutdown shutdown waits for all tasks to reach terminal state before joining threads (I10)

Injector<T>

pub struct Injector<T> {
    queue: crossbeam_deque::Injector<T>,   // or equivalent MPMC impl
}
impl<T: Send> Injector<T> {
    pub fn push(&self, val: T);
    pub fn steal(&self) -> Steal<T>;
    pub fn is_empty(&self) -> bool;
}
  • Global MPMC overflow queue; workers steal from it when local deque + stealers empty.
  • One global injector plus optional per-NUMA-domain injectors (Phase 9).

11.5 pcs-graph — TaskGraph, GraphNode, Dependency

NodeId

#[derive(Copy, Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub struct NodeId(pub u32);

GraphNode

pub struct GraphNode {
    pub task:         Task,
    pub pending_deps: AtomicUsize,          // count of unsatisfied in-edges
    pub successors:   Mutex<Vec<NodeId>>,   // edges to decrement on completion
}
  • pending_deps == 0 → node is Ready; pushed to a worker deque (I5).
  • successors locked only during graph construction and completion notification; not on hot path.

Dependency

pub struct Dependency {
    pub pred: NodeId,
    pub succ: NodeId,
}

TaskGraph

pub struct TaskGraph {
    nodes:    Vec<GraphNode>,             // indexed by NodeId
    pool:     Arc<ThreadPool>,
    complete: Latch,                      // signaled when all nodes Done/Panicked
}
impl TaskGraph {
    pub fn add_node(&mut self, task: Task) -> NodeId;
    pub fn add_edge(&mut self, pred: NodeId, succ: NodeId) -> Result<(), CycleError>;
    pub fn submit(self);                  // validate (DFS cycle check), push Ready nodes, wait
    pub fn wait(self);                    // block until complete Latch fires
}
Property Detail
Cycle detection DFS on add_edge; returns CycleError immediately
Completion each worker decrements pending_deps of successors on task Done; pushes those hitting 0 (I5)
Locality successor pushed to the completing worker's local deque (Phase 5, Phase 9)

11.6 pcs-parallel — Scope, ParallelIterator, Producer

Scope<'s>

pub struct Scope<'s> {
    pool:    &'s ThreadPool,
    latch:   Arc<Latch>,
    panics:  Arc<Mutex<Vec<Box<dyn Any + Send>>>>,
    _marker: PhantomData<&'s mut &'s ()>,
}
impl<'s> Scope<'s> {
    pub fn spawn<F: FnOnce() + Send + 's>(&self, f: F);
}
// Drop impl: blocks until latch reaches 0; re-raises first captured panic (I7, I8)

join<A, B, RA, RB>

pub fn join<A, B, RA, RB>(a: A, b: B) -> (RA, RB)
where
    A: FnOnce() -> RA + Send,
    B: FnOnce() -> RB + Send,
    RA: Send,
    RB: Send;
  • Push b to local deque; run a inline; pop-or-steal b (work-first, Phase 4).
  • Both results available before return (I4).

Producer

pub trait Producer: Send + Sized {
    type Item: Send;
    fn split_at(self, index: usize) -> (Self, Self);
    fn len(&self) -> usize;
    fn into_iter(self) -> impl Iterator<Item = Self::Item>;
}

ParallelIterator

pub trait ParallelIterator: Sized {
    type Item: Send;
    fn for_each<F: Fn(Self::Item) + Send + Sync>(self, f: F);
    fn map<B: Send, F: Fn(Self::Item) -> B + Send + Sync>(self, f: F) -> impl ParallelIterator<Item = B>;
    fn reduce<F: Fn(Self::Item, Self::Item) -> Self::Item + Send + Sync>(self, identity: Self::Item, f: F) -> Self::Item;
    fn scan<B: Send, F: Fn(&B, Self::Item) -> B + Send + Sync>(self, init: B, f: F) -> Vec<B>;
}
  • reduce result == sequential left-fold for associative f (I9; float reassociation documented).
  • Recursive halving terminates at granularity threshold (Phase 7, P6).

11.7 pcs-sched — SchedPolicy

SchedPolicy

#[derive(Copy, Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum SchedPolicy {
    WorkFirst,      // push continuation to deque; run spawned child immediately
    HelpFirst,      // run continuation inline; push child for steal
    LocalityAware,  // NUMA-biased victim ordering; blends work-first default
}
Variant Cache behavior Steal rate Tail latency
WorkFirst child reuses parent cache; cache-hot LIFO lower lower
HelpFirst continuation near call site higher may increase
LocalityAware within-domain steal first; cross-domain last domain-local low balanced

11.8 pcs-sync — Future<T>, Promise<T>, Latch, Barrier, WaitGroup

Future<T> / Promise<T>

pub struct Promise<T: Send> {
    inner: Arc<SharedSlot<T>>,
}
pub struct Future<T: Send> {
    inner: Arc<SharedSlot<T>>,
}
impl<T: Send> Promise<T> {
    pub fn fulfill(self, val: T);
    pub fn fulfill_panic(self, payload: Box<dyn Any + Send>);
}
impl<T: Send> Future<T> {
    pub fn wait(self) -> T;   // participates in scheduling; does not park thread (P7)
    pub fn is_ready(&self) -> bool;
}
pub fn future_promise<T: Send>() -> (Future<T>, Promise<T>);
  • Future::wait runs other tasks from the local deque while waiting (Phase 8).
  • Thread is never OS-blocked in wait; prevents starvation when all workers wait.

Latch

pub struct Latch {
    count:  AtomicUsize,
    parked: Mutex<Vec<Thread>>,
}
impl Latch {
    pub fn new(n: usize) -> Self;
    pub fn count_down(&self);     // decrement; unpark waiters when 0
    pub fn wait(&self);           // park until count == 0
    pub fn is_zero(&self) -> bool;
}

Barrier

pub struct Barrier {
    n:       usize,
    arrived: AtomicUsize,
    latch:   Latch,
}
impl Barrier {
    pub fn new(n: usize) -> Self;
    pub fn wait(&self);   // last arrival resets + releases all
}

WaitGroup

pub struct WaitGroup {
    count: AtomicIsize,
    latch: Arc<Latch>,
}
impl WaitGroup {
    pub fn new() -> Self;
    pub fn add(&self, delta: isize);
    pub fn done(&self);      // add(-1)
    pub fn wait(&self);      // block until count == 0
}

11.9 pcs-numa — NumaNode, NumaDomain

NumaNode

#[derive(Copy, Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq, Hash, PartialOrd, Ord)]
pub struct NumaNode(pub u8);

NumaDomain

pub struct NumaDomain {
    pub id:       NumaNode,
    pub cpus:     CpuSet,
    pub mem_size: u64,          // bytes of memory on this domain
}

Topology

pub struct Topology {
    pub domains:  Vec<NumaDomain>,
    pub cpu_to_node: Vec<NumaNode>,    // indexed by logical CPU id
}
impl Topology {
    pub fn detect() -> Self;           // reads /sys or libnuma; fallback: single domain
    pub fn node_for_cpu(&self, cpu: usize) -> NumaNode;
    pub fn cpus_for_node(&self, node: NumaNode) -> &CpuSet;
}

11.10 pcs-metrics — StealStats, SchedMetrics

StealStats

pub struct StealStats {
    pub steals_attempted: AtomicU64,
    pub steals_won:       AtomicU64,
    pub tasks_run:        AtomicU64,
    pub park_count:       AtomicU64,
    pub idle_ns:          AtomicU64,
}

SchedMetrics

pub struct SchedMetrics {
    pub per_worker:    Box<[StealStats]>,       // indexed by worker id
    pub global_tasks:  AtomicU64,
    pub latency_hist:  LatencyHistogram,        // fixed-bucket ns histogram
    pub queue_depth:   Box<[AtomicUsize]>,      // per-worker deque depth snapshot
}
impl SchedMetrics {
    pub fn snapshot(&self) -> MetricsSnapshot;  // lock-free read; point-in-time copy
    pub fn reset(&self);
}

LatencyHistogram

pub struct LatencyHistogram {
    buckets: [AtomicU64; 32],   // log2 ns buckets: 0..=1ns, 1..=2ns, 2..=4ns, ...
}
impl LatencyHistogram {
    pub fn record(&self, ns: u64);
    pub fn percentile(&self, p: f64) -> u64;   // approximate; linear interp within bucket
}

11.11 Cross-Crate Type Compatibility Rules

Rule Constraint
Stable ABI fields TaskId, TaskState, NodeId, NumaNode — repr(u64/u8/u32/u8); no reorder
Sealed traits Producer, ParallelIterator — sealed (pub(crate) supertrait); no external impls
Error enums additive only; #[non_exhaustive] on all public error enums
Feature flags numa, tracing, determinism — additive; default features exclude numa
MSRV Rust 1.75 stable; no nightly-only features in public API
Semver 0.x during phases 1–13; 1.0 after Phase 14 conformance suite passes
unsafe surface only in pcs-deque (deque ops), pcs-numa (affinity syscalls), pcs-core (RawJob); all other crates are safe Rust

Phase 1 — Thread Pool + Worker Threads + Topology

1.1 Objectives

  • Spawn exactly PoolConfig.num_threads OS threads; pin each to a CpuSet derived from hardware topology.
  • Detect cores, physical packages, NUMA nodes via /sys/devices/system/cpu (Linux) or libc::sysconf / mach_vm (macOS).
  • Establish thread-local CURRENT_WORKER: Option<*mut Worker> accessible without synchronization on the hot path.
  • Deliver clean startup (all threads ready before ThreadPool::new returns) and clean shutdown (all threads joined, no dangling handles).
  • Main thread may participate as worker-0 when PoolConfig.main_participates = true.
  • Introduce PoolConfig, ThreadPool, Worker (skeleton), NumaNode, CpuSet, Injector<Task> in pcs-pool and pcs-worker.

1.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

Crates/files touched: pcs-pool/src/lib.rs, pcs-pool/src/config.rs, pcs-pool/src/topology.rs, pcs-worker/src/lib.rs, pcs-worker/src/thread_local.rs.

Step Module Action Theory Anchor
Topology detection topology.rs Parse /sys/devices/system/cpu/cpu*/topology/{core_id,physical_package_id,node} into Vec<NumaNode>; fallback to num_cpus crate count. S3 (sched_setaffinity man); S4 (hwloc); ADR-09
CpuSet binding topology.rs Construct CpuSet bitmask per worker; call libc::sched_setaffinity (Linux) / thread_policy_set (macOS) immediately after thread spawn. P4 (never oversubscribe); S3; ADR-10
Worker skeleton pcs-worker/src/lib.rs Worker { id: usize, local: ChaseLevDeque<Task>, stealers: Box<[Stealer<Task>]>, rng: XorShift64, numa: NumaNode, parked: AtomicBool } — deque placeholder is a VecDeque until Phase 2 swaps it. I1 (single owner); I6 (pool not > num_threads); ADR-08 (XorShift)
Thread-local handle thread_local.rs thread_local! { static WORKER: Cell<*mut Worker> = Cell::new(null_mut()); } Set on thread entry; cleared on exit. P8 (no global sync on steal path); B2 (Ch.22 raw-pointer discipline)
Startup barrier pcs-pool/src/lib.rs Arc<Barrier> with count = num_threads + 1; each worker thread waits; main thread waits last — ThreadPool::new returns after all workers are pinned and ready. B9 (Ch.26 condvar predicate re-check); S5 (POSIX pthread_create)
Injector pcs-pool/src/lib.rs Injector<Task> wraps a crossbeam-deque::Injector or a hand-rolled MPMC queue; used for external submissions and overflow. P-J (MS-queue); ADR-06; P10 (backpressure at boundary)
Shutdown pcs-pool/src/lib.rs AtomicBool shutdown_flag; set in ThreadPool::drop; each worker checks flag after each task and on park wake; park implementation uses Condvar per-worker (Phase 3 refines to futex). I10 (shutdown: all tasks terminal); ADR-07 (park mechanism)
PoolConfig config.rs PoolConfig { num_threads: usize, stack_size: usize, affinity: AffinityMode, policy: SchedPolicy, main_participates: bool } with Default impl using num_cpus::get(). P4; ADR-01 through ADR-03; P-B (worker count sizing bound)

Stealers exchange: after all workers are created, collect worker.local.stealer() for each worker, distribute full Box<[Stealer]> to every other worker, then release startup barrier.

1.3 Deliverable

  • ThreadPool::new(PoolConfig) compiles and spawns N pinned threads.
  • ThreadPool::shutdown() / Drop joins all handles; returns cleanly.
  • pcs-pool exports: ThreadPool, PoolConfig, Worker (opaque), topology::detect() -> Vec<NumaNode>.
  • CURRENT_WORKER readable from any worker thread; None on external threads.
  • Integration test: spawn pool, verify thread count, verify affinity via /proc/self/status Cpus_allowed.

1.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
Startup latency (N=16) < 20 ms wall time
Thread count Exactly PoolConfig.num_threads threads alive during pool lifetime
Affinity Each thread's sched_getaffinity bitmask matches its assigned CpuSet
Clean shutdown All threads joined, no zombie handles, Drop returns in < 50 ms
CURRENT_WORKER Returns Some on every worker thread; None on main thread (when main_participates = false)
Topology test detect() returns num_threads distinct CPU ids on CI (mocked if bare metal unavailable)

1.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose Expected output
tests/pool_startup.rs Spawn + join, verify thread count Pass, 0 panics
tests/affinity_check.rs Read /proc/self/status per thread Cpus_allowed matches mask (Linux only; skipped on macOS)
tests/main_participates.rs main_participates=true, submit a task, check it runs on WORKER thread-local Task runs, CURRENT_WORKER is Some
tests/topology_smoke.rs detect() on CI worker Returns >= 1 NUMA node, >= 1 CPU
tests/shutdown_clean.rs Drop pool; verify no thread is alive after drop Pass via Arc<AtomicUsize> thread-alive counter

1.6 Pitfalls

  • Stealer distribution before barrier release: if workers begin stealing before all Stealer handles are distributed, a worker has a partial stealers array. Fix: distribute stealers before releasing startup barrier.
  • Affinity fallback on macOS: sched_setaffinity absent; silently skip or use pthread_setaffinity_np where available; topology detection must not panic on unsupported syscalls.
  • Stack size mismatch: default Rust thread stack (2 MB) insufficient for deep recursive fork-join. PoolConfig.stack_size must be passed to std::thread::Builder::stack_size.
  • Thread-local raw pointer lifetime: WORKER: Cell<*mut Worker> is valid only during thread body; accessing after thread exit is UB. Clear on thread exit before joining.
  • NUMA node count = 0: some VMs expose no NUMA topology; treat as single node, not a panic.
  • Shutdown while tasks are inflight (Phase 1 skeleton): workers must drain local deques before exiting; stubs must not silently discard tasks. Assert empty in test.

1.7 Performance Concerns

  • sched_setaffinity syscall done once at thread start; negligible cost thereafter.
  • Arc<Barrier> allocates; use a stack-allocated latch for startup-only barrier if minimizing heap pressure.
  • Thread-local access pattern: Cell<*mut Worker> is a single load with no synchronization — zero overhead on hot path.
  • num_cpus::get() may invoke sysconf(_SC_NPROCESSORS_ONLN); cache result in PoolConfig.
  • Startup barrier wait() spins before parking; spin count bounded by num_threads * spawn_overhead.

1.8 Edge Cases

  • num_threads = 0: rejected in PoolConfig::validate() with Err.
  • num_threads = 1: single worker, no stealing possible; must still compile and run correctly.
  • num_threads > available CPUs: over-subscription; affinity masks overlap; log warning, do not panic.
  • stack_size = 0: default to OS default (platform-dependent minimum).
  • main_participates = true with external ThreadPool::new call on a worker thread: disallow recursion (detect via CURRENT_WORKER).
  • Pool dropped while main_participates worker is running a task: shutdown must wait for that task to complete.

1.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery
Thread spawn fails (resource limits) std::thread::Builder::spawn returns Err Pool creation fails; no partial state ThreadPool::new returns Err; caller must retry or reduce num_threads
Affinity syscall fails Non-zero errno Thread runs without pinning Log warning; continue without affinity
Startup barrier deadlock Timeout on barrier wait All threads blocked; process hangs Add timeout to barrier wait; return Err from new
Thread-local corruption UB dereference, segfault Undefined Sanitizer (AddressSanitizer) in CI; raw pointer clearing on exit

1.10 Operational Notes

  • pcs-metrics counter pool_threads_spawned: AtomicUsize incremented per thread; exported via SchedMetrics::snapshot().
  • Log (tracing INFO) on pool creation: num_threads, numa_nodes, affinity_mode.
  • Log DEBUG per thread: worker_id, cpu_id, numa_node.
  • No runtime reconfiguration of thread count in Phase 1; pool is immutable after construction.
  • CI must run topology detection tests with --test-threads=1 to avoid affinity mask conflicts between tests.

Phase 2 — Chase-Lev Work-Stealing Deque

2.1 Objectives

  • Implement ChaseLevDeque<T> in pcs-deque: lock-free circular buffer; owner push/pop wait-free; thief steal via CAS on top.
  • Implement Stealer<T>: cloneable handle; exposes only steal() -> Steal<T>.
  • Correct C11/Rust memory orderings per Le, Pop, Cohen, Zappa Nardelli (PPoPP 2013) for weak memory models.
  • Monotonic top counter defeats ABA; growable buffer via buffer swap.
  • Replace Phase 1's VecDeque placeholder in Worker with ChaseLevDeque<Task>.

2.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: P-A (Chase-Lev 2005); P-C (Lê et al. PPoPP 2013 orderings); P-N (Treiber ABA); B1 Ch.16; B3 Ch.7; S1; S6 (align(64)); I1; I2; I3 -->

Crates/files: pcs-deque/src/lib.rs, pcs-deque/src/buffer.rs, pcs-deque/src/stealer.rs.

Data layout:

ChaseLevDeque<T>:
  bottom: AtomicUsize   -- owner's write index (push increments, pop decrements)
  top:    AtomicUsize   -- thieves' read index (steal increments via CAS); monotonically increasing
  buffer: AtomicPtr<Buffer<T>>  -- pointer to current circular buffer; swapped on grow
Buffer<T>:
  cap: usize            -- power-of-two capacity
  data: [UnsafeCell<MaybeUninit<T>>; cap]
  mask: usize           -- cap - 1
  prev: *mut Buffer<T>  -- singly-linked list for deferred dealloc

Algorithms (described; no bodies):

Operation Actor Memory ordering summary (per Lê et al Table 1)
push(t) Owner Write data[bottom & mask]; Release store of bottom.
pop() -> Option<T> Owner Load bottom - 1 (Relaxed); store bottom - 1 (Relaxed); load top (SeqCst); if bottom - 1 < top restore and return None; else CAS top if contested (AcqRel).
steal() -> Steal<T> Thief Load top (Acquire); load bottom (Acquire); if empty return Empty; load data[top & mask]; CAS top old→old+1 (AcqRel); on failure return Retry; on success return Success(t).
grow() Owner Allocate new buffer (2× cap); copy live entries; Release store of buffer pointer; link old buffer into prev chain for safe dealloc after quiescence.

Buffer deallocation: old buffers not freed immediately (thief may still hold reference); collect into prev chain; free when top advances past the old buffer's last slot (no live references possible).

Steal<T> enum: { Success(T), Empty, Retry } — caller retries on Retry; bounded retry count before giving up.

Stealer<T>: holds Arc<Inner<T>> (shared with ChaseLevDeque); Inner contains the atomic fields; Stealer exposes only steal() -> Steal<T>.

Integration: Worker::local field changes from VecDeque<Task> to ChaseLevDeque<Task>; Worker::stealer() returns Stealer<Task> for distribution to peers.

2.3 Deliverable

  • pcs-deque compiles with #![forbid(unsafe_code)] disabled (unsafe needed), Miri-clean single-threaded, loom-verified concurrent paths.
  • Stealer<Task> distributed to all workers at pool startup (Phase 1 integration complete).
  • pop() returns None on empty deque; push + pop round-trip is correct LIFO under single-threaded use.
  • Multi-threaded steal-under-contention test passes (≥ 1000 steal races, no item lost or doubled).

2.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
Single-thread LIFO Push 10k items, pop all; LIFO order verified
No item lost Push N items, steal all from M threads; sum of recovered items = N
No double execution Each item recovered exactly once across all threads
ABA safety top never wraps within a test run of 1M operations (monotonic counter)
loom model check loom::sync::atomic sweep covers push/steal race; no assertion failures
Miri cargo miri test clean under pcs-deque unit tests
Grow correctness Push beyond initial capacity; all items recoverable after buffer swap

2.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose Expected output
tests/deque_lifo.rs Single-thread push/pop order Exact LIFO sequence
tests/deque_steal_fifo.rs Single owner push N; single thief steals all FIFO order of stolen items; none lost
tests/deque_concurrent.rs 1 owner pushing, M thieves stealing simultaneously sum(stolen) + sum(popped) = N
tests/deque_grow.rs Push 2× initial capacity; pop all All items recovered, correct order
tests/deque_loom.rs loom-instrumented push/steal/pop 3-op interleaving No invariant violation
tests/deque_empty.rs pop/steal on empty deque Returns None / Steal::Empty respectively

2.6 Pitfalls

  • Wrong memory ordering on pop: reading top after decrementing bottom without SeqCst allows a thief to simultaneously succeed the CAS; results in double execution. Must follow Lê et al Table 1 exactly.
  • Freeing buffer while thief holds pointer: thief loads buffer pointer, then owner grows and frees old buffer before thief reads data[top & mask]. Fix: deferred free via prev chain; only free when top has passed the old buffer's last index.
  • Capacity not power-of-two: mask = cap - 1 index trick is undefined for non-power-of-two sizes; validate in Buffer::new.
  • Steal::Retry infinite loop in test: callers must bound retry count; a test that spins forever on Retry will hang CI. Use a retry limit + thread::yield_now().
  • usize overflow of bottom/top: on 64-bit targets, usize::MAX wrap is astronomically unlikely but must be documented; assert in debug builds that bottom - top < isize::MAX.
  • Concurrent grow not possible: only the owner may grow; document that push panics in debug if called concurrently (but this is architecturally enforced by single-owner contract).

2.7 Performance Concerns

  • push and pop (no contention): 2–3 atomic ops, no CAS; should be < 10 ns per op on modern hardware.
  • steal CAS failure rate: under high contention (many thieves, small deque), CAS retries add latency; mitigate with Steal::Retry returning control to the outer steal loop, which may select a different victim.
  • Buffer grow: O(cap) copy; amortized O(1) per push; avoid growing under hot-path conditions by pre-sizing with PoolConfig hint.
  • False sharing between bottom and top: place on separate cache lines (#[repr(align(64))] on a wrapper struct).
  • Arc<Inner<T>> allocation per deque: one allocation at construction; negligible thereafter.
  • AtomicPtr<Buffer<T>> load on every pop/steal: single acquire load; keep hot in L1 by accessing frequently.

2.8 Edge Cases

  • Deque capacity 1: push 1, pop 1 — must work; push 2 triggers grow.
  • Owner and single thief race on the last element: pop and steal both attempt; exactly one wins via CAS; the other gets None / Retry.
  • steal called after owner has popped the same element: bottom == top before CAS; steal returns Empty.
  • Buffer pointer swap races with thief's data read: covered by deferred-free protocol (2.2); must be exercised in loom test.
  • T = Task where Task contains Box<dyn FnOnce()>: MaybeUninit<Task> read without init is UB; loom + Miri will catch premature reads.

2.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery
Double execution of a task Task side-effects observed twice Incorrect results, potential UB Invariant I1/I3 violated; root-cause via loom under wrong ordering
Lost task sum(popped+stolen) < N in conformance test Task silently dropped Audit memory orderings against Lê et al; add SeqCst fence
Use-after-free on buffer Miri / ASan report Memory corruption Implement deferred-free protocol; verify in Miri
Deque capacity overflow bottom wraps past top Incorrect indices Add debug assertion; pre-size buffer adequately

2.10 Operational Notes

  • pcs-metrics exposes per-worker deque_depth: AtomicUsize sampled after each push; used by Phase 13 flamegraph and depth gauges.
  • pcs-deque has zero dependencies beyond std; feature-gated loom dependency for model-check tests (#[cfg(loom)]).
  • loom tests run under cargo test --features loom; not in default CI profile (too slow); run in nightly CI job.
  • Document the memory ordering rationale inline per operation with citation to Lê et al section numbers.
  • Expose ChaseLevDeque::len_hint() -> usize (non-atomic snapshot, approximate) for metrics sampling.

Phase 3 — Task Model + Spawn + Steal Loop

3.1 Objectives

  • Define Task, TaskId, TaskState in pcs-core; TaskState transitions via AtomicTaskState.
  • Implement spawn on ThreadPool and Scope (Scope is a stub here; completed in Phase 4): push Task to current worker's local deque; if called from external thread, push to Injector.
  • Implement the worker run loop in pcs-worker: pop-local (LIFO) → steal-victim (round-robin + RNG) → park; unpark on new work; exponential backoff before parking.
  • Integrate ChaseLevDeque<Task> (Phase 2) and ThreadPool (Phase 1) into a functioning end-to-end task execution path.

3.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: P-E (Arora et al. steal loop analysis); P-B (T1/P + T∞ bound, worker count); P-J (MS-queue Injector); B9 Ch.26 (park predicate); ADR-07 (park); ADR-08 (XorShift); I1; I3; P1; P8 -->

Crates/files: pcs-core/src/lib.rs, pcs-core/src/task.rs, pcs-core/src/task_id.rs, pcs-worker/src/loop_.rs, pcs-pool/src/spawn.rs.

Types:

TaskId: u64 -- generated by AtomicU64 counter in pcs-core; wrapping add; 0 reserved as sentinel
TaskState: enum { Pending, Ready, Running, Done, Panicked }
AtomicTaskState: wraps AtomicU8 with encode/decode; transitions are CAS
Task: { id: TaskId, run: Box<dyn FnOnce() + Send + 'static>, state: AtomicTaskState }

State transitions:

Pending → Ready   (deps satisfied; Phase 5)
Ready   → Running (worker pops/steals and executes)
Running → Done    (run() returns normally)
Running → Panicked (run() unwinds, caught via std::panic::catch_unwind)

Spawn path:

Context Action
Worker thread (CURRENT_WORKER is Some) worker.local.push(task); unpark one idle sibling if deque was empty
External thread pool.injector.push(task); unpark one idle worker

Worker run loop (pcs-worker/src/loop_.rs): each iteration checks the pool shutdown flag first and exits the loop if set. Otherwise it tries a local pop; a hit executes the task and restarts the loop. Failing that, it tries try_steal(); a hit executes the stolen task and restarts the loop. Only when both the local pop and every steal attempt come back empty does the worker fall through to backoff(), which spins, then yields, then parks the OS thread — in that order, with the loop restarting from the top on every wake.

Steal-loop sub-behaviors:

Component Behavior Anchor
try_steal() Select victim rng.next() % num_workers; try up to num_workers victims. stealers[victim].steal() → Retry: next victim; Empty: skip; Success(t): return Some(t). Records steal_attempted/steal_won in StealStats. P-E
Backoff before park spin_count: u32 doubles each idle iteration, capped at MAX_SPIN = 256; then one thread::yield_now(); then park() via per-worker Parker (Condvar+Mutex<bool>); Parker::unpark() callable from any thread's spawn path. ADR-07
execute(task) CAS state Ready→Running (log+skip if it fails); `catch_unwind(
External ThreadPool::submit(f) -> TaskId Construct Task; push to Injector; unpark one idle worker; return TaskId. P-J
Worker drains Injector After exhausting the local deque and before stealing peers: injector.steal() once; insert into local deque or execute directly. P-J

3.3 Deliverable

  • ThreadPool::submit(|| ...) accepted from the main thread; task executes on a worker; result observable via shared AtomicUsize or Future stub.
  • Worker loop runs indefinitely until shutdown; does not busy-spin when idle (parks correctly).
  • Steal path exercised by submitting more tasks than one worker can consume alone.
  • TaskState transitions Ready → Running → Done observable in integration test.

3.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
Correctness Submit 10k tasks; all reach Done; none lost or double-executed
No busy-wait at idle CPU utilization < 2% when pool is idle for 1 s (measured via /proc/PID/stat)
Steal observed With 1 producer + N-1 idle workers, steal_won > 0 after submitting 100 tasks
Park/unpark latency Task submission to execution start < 500 µs on idle pool (N=8)
Shutdown All tasks submitted before shutdown() are Done when shutdown() returns
No panic leak A panicking task transitions to Panicked; pool continues running other tasks

3.5 Conformance Fixtures

File Purpose Expected output
tests/submit_done.rs Submit N tasks; await all Done via AtomicUsize latch Counter == N
tests/steal_observed.rs 1-thread submit burst; multi-worker pool; check StealStats steal_won > 0
tests/idle_cpu.rs Idle pool for 1 s; sample CPU time < 2% CPU on Linux (skipped on macOS)
tests/panic_task.rs Submit a panicking task; submit 5 normal tasks after 5 normal tasks complete; pool alive
tests/taskstate_trace.rs Instrument AtomicTaskState; verify sequence Each task: Ready→Running→Done
tests/external_submit.rs Submit from non-worker thread Task executes on a worker thread (verify via CURRENT_WORKER)

3.6 Pitfalls

  • Spin-before-park CPU waste: if MAX_SPIN is too high (e.g., 1M), idle workers consume a full core. Start conservatively at 64–256 spins; tune with idle-CPU test.
  • Injector drain ordering: workers must check Injector after exhausting local deque but before sleeping; if they check only on wake, injector-submitted tasks are delayed until a steal wakes a worker.
  • Unpark storm: submitting N tasks from an external thread calls unpark() N times; if all N workers wake simultaneously and find only one item, N-1 re-park immediately — this is correct but causes unnecessary wakeups. Mitigate by unparking only one worker per submission.
  • catch_unwind on non-unwind panics: panic = "abort" (common in embedded/WASM profiles) makes catch_unwind useless; document that panic = "unwind" is required in Cargo.toml for the pool crate.
  • Box<dyn FnOnce()> size: each Task heap-allocates the closure; many tiny closures → allocator pressure. Future optimization (Phase 6+): task arena or stack-allocated small-task optimization.
  • TaskId counter wrap: AtomicU64::fetch_add wraps at u64::MAX; 0 is sentinel; add debug assertion in TaskId::new that result != 0 after wrap.

3.7 Performance Concerns

  • Box<dyn FnOnce() + Send> — vtable dispatch on run(): one indirect call per task; acceptable; inlining not possible.
  • catch_unwind: zero cost on the non-panicking path (LLVM personality function only invoked on unwind); do not skip for performance.
  • AtomicTaskState CAS on every state transition: Running → Done is uncontested (single writer); use store(Relaxed) for Done write if no other thread reads state concurrently (relax where safe per invariant I1).
  • Per-task TaskId allocation via AtomicU64::fetch_add(SeqCst): relax to Relaxed + fetch_add; counter monotonicity does not require SeqCst.
  • try_steal iterates up to num_workers stealers per idle round: cap at min(num_workers, 8) attempts before backing off to avoid O(N) scan on large pools.
  • Parker Condvar: wakeup latency ~5–30 µs; acceptable for task granularity > 100 µs (P6); for fine-grained tasks, futex-based Parker is faster (Phase 7 refinement).

3.8 Edge Cases

  • Submit task that immediately submits child tasks (recursive spawn): child pushes to worker's local deque while parent is running; deque must not be in an inconsistent state during push from inside execute.
  • Submit after shutdown() called: ThreadPool::submit must return Err(PoolShutdown) or panic with clear message.
  • All workers park simultaneously when last task completes: next submit must unpark at least one worker promptly.
  • Task closure captures !Send type (e.g., Rc): trait bound Send on Task.run rejects at compile time; no runtime concern.
  • Zero-task burst (empty pool): workers park immediately; no spin; test with thread::sleep and CPU check.
  • Task that calls std::process::exit: not catchable; documented as caller responsibility.

3.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery
Task never executes (stuck in Injector) tasks_run counter does not increase; integration test timeout Tasks silently queued forever Audit injector.steal() placement in worker loop; ensure unpark is called
Worker thread exits unexpectedly Arc<AtomicUsize> alive_count decremented; checked in shutdown Reduced parallelism; possible deadlock if join waits Log error; ThreadPool::health_check() returns Err; Phase 12 adds replacement
Panic payload lost (pre-Phase 11) Panicking task state = Panicked; payload not re-raised Caller unaware of child failure Phase 11 completes propagation; in Phase 3, log ERROR with debug info
Busy spin on shutdown Worker ignores shutdown flag in spin loop CPU saturated after shutdown Assert shutdown check is inside spin loop, not only in park check

3.10 Operational Notes

  • StealStats fields (steals_attempted, steals_won, tasks_run, park_count, idle_ns): all AtomicU64; per-worker; aggregated in SchedMetrics::snapshot().
  • pcs-metrics increments idle_ns by measuring Instant delta between park enter and wake.
  • tracing TRACE span per task execute: task_id, worker_id, source = {local|steal|injector}.
  • Logging levels: INFO for pool lifecycle; DEBUG for steal events; TRACE for per-task state transitions. Default filter WARN in production to avoid log overhead.
  • No dynamic reconfiguration of num_threads in Phase 3; pool size is fixed at construction.
  • SchedMetrics::reset() available for benchmark harness to clear counters between runs.

Phase 4 — Fork-join (scope/join)

4.1 Objectives

  • Implement join(a, b) in pcs-parallel: push b to local deque, run a inline, then pop-or-steal b (work-first continuation).
  • Implement Scope<'s> in pcs-parallel: borrow-scoped task spawning, lifetime 's prevents scope outliving spawned closures.
  • Guarantee I4: join returns only after both tasks are Done.
  • Guarantee I7: panics captured per-task, re-raised at join/scope boundary.
  • Guarantee I8: Scope does not return until all scope-spawned tasks reach terminal state.
  • Expose recursive divide-and-conquer pattern; no unsafe unless deque ABI requires it.

4.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: P-F (Cilk-5 work-first, THE protocol); P-D (Acar work-first vs help-first); B2 Ch.19 (Scope<'s> PhantomData invariance); S8 (catch_unwind UnwindSafe); I4; I7; I8; P5; ADR-21 -->

Module: pcs-parallel/src/join.rs, pcs-parallel/src/scope.rs

join(a, b) algorithm:

  1. Wrap b in a Task; push to current worker's local deque via worker::with_current(|w| w.local.push(b_task)).
  2. Execute a inline on calling thread.
  3. Attempt worker::with_current(|w| w.local.pop()) to retrieve b_task (LIFO); if successful, run it locally.
  4. If pop fails (b stolen): busy-poll b_future.wait() — internally runs other tasks from local deque to avoid idle-spin (see Phase 8 Future::wait, referenced Phase 8).
  5. After both complete, check both TaskState for Panicked; if either, re-raise stored panic payload via std::panic::resume_unwind.

Scope<'s> structure:

pub struct Scope<'s> {
    latch: Arc<Latch>,
    panic_store: Mutex<Option<Box<dyn Any + Send>>>,
    _marker: PhantomData<&'s ()>,
}
  • Scope::spawn(&self, f: impl FnOnce() + Send + 's): wraps f in a Task, increments latch count, pushes to worker deque; on task Done decrements latch and stores panic if any.
  • scope(pool, |s| { s.spawn(…); }): calls closure, then latch.wait() (participates in scheduling — no thread block), then checks panic_store and re-raises.
  • Lifetime variance: 's is invariant on Scope; closures borrow at most 's data, preventing use-after-free.

Panic capture: wrap each task body in std::panic::catch_unwind; store Box<dyn Any + Send> in Task.panic_slot: Option<Box<dyn Any + Send>>; transition state to Panicked.

Latch: pcs-sync Latch — counter AtomicUsize, on decrement-to-zero unparks waiter; waiter participates in work loop instead of sleeping.

Recursive pattern: helper join_recursive(slice, depth_limit) splits at midpoint, calls join(left_half, right_half) — no scheduler-specific code; composable.

4.3 Deliverable

  • join(a, b) compiles and runs; parallel mergesort fixture sorts correctly and verifiably matches sequential output.
  • scope blocks until all spawned tasks complete; borrows enforced at compile time.
  • Panics from child tasks propagate to caller.
  • pcs-cli benchmark: parallel mergesort on 1M integers, speedup reported.

4.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
join correctness 100% match to sequential result on mergesort fixture
Scope lifetime enforced compile-error if scope outlives borrow
Panic propagation 100% re-raise on panic_in_child fixture
No task double-execution I1 holds; tasks_run counter == spawned count
join speedup (8 cores) ≥ 5× vs sequential on 1M-element sort
No deque underflow/overflow 10k-iteration stress, no assertion failures

4.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected
join_mergesort_1m.rs correctness + speedup sorted output == sequential
join_single_elem.rs degenerate: 1-element join no deadlock, correct result
scope_borrow_lifetime.rs lifetime compile rejection must not compile
panic_in_child.rs panic propagation std::panic re-raised at join
panic_both_children.rs both arms panic exactly one panic re-raised, no double-panic
scope_nested.rs nested scopes inner scope completes before outer
join_stolen_b.rs b is stolen before a completes still joins correctly
join_stress_1k.rs 1k concurrent join trees no leak, no double-run (I1, I3)

4.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug Signal Recovery
b re-stolen after pop attempt fails b runs twice pop/steal race without atomic transfer tasks_run > spawned CAS-guarded steal (I1, I2)
Panic payload dropped silent failure after panic catch_unwind result discarded TaskState stuck at Panicked store payload in Task.panic_slot
Scope lifetime unsound use-after-free 's variance error UB / MIRI report invariant PhantomData<&'s ()>
Latch wait spins forever hang latch counter miscounted idle_ns metric spike decrement in finally/drop; verify increment before push
Recursive join stack overflow SIGSEGV unbounded recursion depth stack size exceeded depth_limit parameter; stop splitting at threshold
Panic not re-raised in join silent swallow forgot to check panic_slot test produces wrong result without panic explicit check after both tasks Done

4.7 Performance Concerns

  • Work-first (run a inline, push b) keeps continuation on local deque → LIFO pop = cache hot (P3).
  • Busy-polling b_future.wait() must not spin-sleep; it must run other local tasks → avoids core idle.
  • catch_unwind on every task body: ~5 ns overhead (negligible vs task body cost); profile only if granularity < 500 ns.
  • Stack depth for deeply recursive divide-and-conquer: each join level = one stack frame; set stack_size in PoolConfig (default 2 MiB per thread).
  • Mutex<Option<Box<…>>> in Scope panic store: cold path only; not on hot task-run path.
  • Latch counter cache line: AtomicUsize on own cache line to avoid false sharing with Scope fields.

4.8 Edge Cases

  • join(a, b) where b completes before a returns: pop finds b already Done; must handle gracefully.
  • scope closure spawns zero tasks: latch count stays 0, returns immediately — no hang.
  • Nested join on a non-worker thread (main thread): with_current returns None; fall back to sequential execution or inject into global Injector.
  • join called after ThreadPool shutdown: worker handle invalid; return Err(PoolShutdown).
  • Both arms of join panic: capture first, discard second (or chain); document which is preserved.
  • Recursive split to depth 1 element: leaf task runs single-element no-op; no allocation.

4.9 Failure Analysis

  • Double-execution (I1 violated): tasks_run > spawned; root = missing atomic state guard on steal; detection = assertion in task runner; blast radius = data corruption; recovery = restart, fix CAS in pcs-deque.
  • Latch never zeroes: scope hangs; root = spawn succeeded but decrement on done skipped (e.g., panic path bypassed drop); detection = idle_ns diverges; containment = timeout in test harness; recovery = fix panic path to always decrement.
  • Panic swallowed: wrong results returned silently; root = join does not re-raise; detection = fixture panic_in_child must panic at caller; recovery = enforce panic_slot check.

4.10 Operational Notes

  • pcs-metrics records tasks_run, steals_won per phase; join-heavy workloads show high local-pop rate and low steal rate.
  • pcs-trace emits TaskSpawned(id), TaskDone(id), PanicCapture(id) events; flamegraph shows join tree depth.
  • Toggle SCHED_POLICY=WorkFirst vs HelpFirst via PoolConfig; work-first typically outperforms help-first on divide-and-conquer (P1, P3).
  • No deployment migration: pure in-process; no persistence, no wire format.

Phase 5 — Task graph / DAG

5.1 Objectives

  • Implement TaskGraph in pcs-graph: nodes hold Task + pending_deps: AtomicUsize + successors: Vec<NodeId>.
  • On each task Done, atomically decrement all successor pending_deps; node reaching 0 becomes Ready and is pushed to a worker deque (locality: prefer the completing task's worker).
  • Reject cycles at graph-build time (topological sort or DFS mark).
  • Provide a completion_latch: Latch per graph; caller waits until all terminal nodes done.
  • Uphold I5: a node becomes Ready iff pending_deps == 0.

5.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: B4 Ch.11 (DAG dependency model); P-K (Drebes topology-aware DAG); S10 (OpenMP depend semantics); I5; P-B (work-span on DAG critical path) -->

Module: pcs-graph/src/graph.rs, pcs-graph/src/node.rs, pcs-graph/src/executor.rs

pub struct GraphNode {
    pub id: NodeId,
    pub task: Task,
    pub pending_deps: AtomicUsize,
    pub successors: Vec<NodeId>,
    pub state: AtomicTaskState,
}

pub struct TaskGraph {
    nodes: Vec<GraphNode>,          // indexed by NodeId
    roots: Vec<NodeId>,             // nodes with pending_deps == 0 at build time
    completion_latch: Arc<Latch>,
}

Build + execution steps:

Phase # Step
Build 1 TaskGraph::builder() accepts (id, task, deps: &[NodeId]) for each node.
Build 2 Compute pending_deps = in-degree for each node.
Build 3 Run Kahn's algorithm (or DFS color-marking) to detect cycles; return Err(CycleDetected) on failure.
Build 4 Collect roots (in-degree 0); set completion_latch count = total node count.
Execute 1 TaskGraph::submit(pool) pushes all root nodes as Task into the pool's Injector or directly to the calling worker's local deque.
Execute 2 Each node's task body runs GraphNode::run(); on completion it calls graph.on_complete(node_id, completing_worker_id).
Execute 3 on_complete: transitions node to Done/Panicked; for each succ in node.successors, fetch_sub(1, AcqRel) on succ.pending_deps — if the result was 1 (now 0), pushes succ to worker_id's local deque (locality) or Injector if full; then decrements completion_latch.
Execute 4 TaskGraph::wait(pool) calls completion_latch.wait() with work-loop participation (executor.rs; pcs-graph/src/executor.rs).

NodeId: newtype NodeId(u32) — index into nodes: Vec; no heap pointer chasing per successor. Locality heuristic: prefer pushing ready successor to completing worker's deque (producer affinity); fall back to Injector if deque full or worker parked. Panic handling: if a node panics, mark Panicked, still decrement successors' pending_deps but set a graph-level poisoned: AtomicBool; wait() re-raises captured panic.

5.3 Deliverable

  • TaskGraph::builder().node(id, task, deps).build() compiles and executes correctly.
  • BFS fixture (Phase 14 bench) produces correct traversal order.
  • wait() blocks until all nodes complete; completion latch fires exactly once.
  • Cycle detection returns error at build time, not runtime.

5.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
Correctness (I5) 100% fixture match: node fires only after all deps done
Cycle detection Err(CycleDetected) for all fixture cycle graphs
No double-execution (I1, I3) tasks_run == node count across 10k-run stress
Completion latch accuracy wait() returns iff all nodes Done or Panicked
Panic propagation graph-level panic re-raised at wait()
Locality: push-to-completing-worker ≥ 70% of ready-set pushes land on completing worker's deque in benchmark
DAG throughput (1k-node diamond) ≥ 80% linear speedup at 8 cores

5.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected
dag_linear_chain.rs nodes 0→1→2→…→N sequential order, all done
dag_diamond.rs classic fork-join diamond node 3 fires only after 1 and 2
dag_wide_fanout.rs root fans into 1000 leaves all leaves run in parallel
dag_cycle_triangle.rs 0→1→2→0 Err(CycleDetected) at build
dag_self_loop.rs node depends on itself Err(CycleDetected)
dag_panic_node.rs one node panics wait() re-raises; downstream may not run
dag_empty.rs zero nodes wait() returns immediately
dag_stress_10k_nodes.rs random DAG, 10k nodes no double-run, no hang
dag_bfs_graph.rs BFS on a 100-node graph correct BFS order vs sequential

5.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug Signal Recovery
pending_deps underflow node runs before all deps extra decrement (off-by-one in successor list) pending_deps hits 0 early; wrong result audit successor list build; assert in on_complete
ABA on pending_deps successor fires twice counter wraps or re-used NodeId tasks_run > node count monotonic NodeId; saturating-sub guard
Cycle not detected hang or double-run cycle rejection skipped wait() never returns mandatory cycle check in build(); no build() bypass
Locality push to parked worker extra steal latency worker parked before push arrives steal count spike unpark after push; or use Injector as fallback
Panic in node not propagated silent wrong result poisoned flag not checked in wait() wait() returns Ok always check poisoned after latch fires
Graph node Vec reallocated mid-run data race nodes Vec pushed after submit crash / corruption build graph fully before calling submit(); seal after build

5.7 Performance Concerns

  • pending_deps: AtomicUsize per node: fetch_sub(1, AcqRel) on the completion path — one atomic per successor edge; keep fan-out bounded (< 1000 successors per node for practical graphs).
  • successors: Vec<NodeId>: small-vec optimization (inline 4 successors) reduces allocations for typical narrow DAGs.
  • Locality push: successor pushed to completing worker's deque → cache-hot task data accessible immediately (P3).
  • completion_latch decrement: one fetch_sub per node completion; hot path if many small nodes.
  • NodeId(u32) vs pointer: avoids pointer chasing; 4 bytes per successor edge; fits cache lines.
  • Graph build (Kahn cycle check): O(V+E); acceptable for V ≤ 100k, E ≤ 1M; beyond that, document limit.

5.8 Edge Cases

  • Node with zero successors and zero predecessors (isolated): immediate Ready; decrement latch on completion.
  • All nodes are roots (fully parallel graph): all submitted at once; pool must accept node_count tasks without deadlock.
  • Node completes, successor has multiple predecessors completing concurrently: fetch_sub + compare to 1 is safe; only the decrement that observes old=1 pushes the node; I5 upheld.
  • Graph submitted to pool that is in shutdown: return Err(PoolShutdown), no tasks pushed.
  • NodeId integer overflow (> 2^32 nodes): saturate and return build error.
  • wait() called before submit(): latch count not decremented; hang or assertion failure; document call order.

5.9 Failure Analysis

  • Node fires before deps complete (I5 violated): pending_deps decremented too many times; detection = assertion pending_deps >= 1 before fetch_sub; blast radius = data corruption in downstream task; recovery = audit successor list construction.
  • wait() never returns: latch never reaches zero; root = one node stuck in Running (panic not captured, latch not decremented); detection = idle_ns diverges; containment = per-node timeout in test harness.
  • Silent panic swallow: graph poisons flag set but not checked; detection = fixture dag_panic_node must surface panic; recovery = mandatory poisoned check in wait().

5.10 Operational Notes

  • pcs-metrics exposes dag_nodes_ready, dag_nodes_done, dag_edges_resolved counters; monitor for stalls.
  • pcs-trace emits NodeReady(id), NodeStart(id), NodeDone(id), NodePanic(id); export as flamegraph task tree.
  • TaskGraph is a one-shot object: build → submit → wait → discard; no reset/reuse API in this phase.
  • No persistence; no wire format; in-process only.

Phase 6 — Data-parallel primitives

6.1 Objectives

  • Implement ParallelIterator trait + Producer trait in pcs-parallel (rayon-style split-at + recursive halving).
  • Implement parallel_for, map, reduce (associative), scan (inclusive prefix) as scheduler-driven operations.
  • Granularity threshold: stop splitting when leaf slice length ≤ grain; grain defaults to max(1, total / (num_threads * OVERDECOMP_FACTOR)).
  • Adaptive splitting: monitor steal pressure; increase grain dynamically to reduce over-decomposition (P6).
  • Uphold I9: reduce result == sequential left-fold for associative operators (up to documented float reassociation).

6.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: B4 Ch.7 (reduce/scan parallelism, associativity); B5 Ch.27 (work-span on parallel reduce/scan); P-L (Robison float reduction); P-F (Cilk-5 divide-and-conquer); R8 (Rayon Producer split); I9; P6; ADR-21 -->

Module: pcs-parallel/src/iter.rs, pcs-parallel/src/producer.rs, pcs-parallel/src/primitives.rs

Trait definitions (signatures only):

pub trait Producer: Send + Sized {
    type Item: Send;
    fn split_at(self, index: usize) -> (Self, Self);
    fn len(&self) -> usize;
    fn into_iter(self) -> impl Iterator<Item = Self::Item>;
}

pub trait ParallelIterator: Sized {
    type Item: Send;
    fn into_producer(self) -> impl Producer<Item = Self::Item>;
    // combinators: map, reduce, for_each, scan — each dispatches to primitives
}

Primitive behaviors:

Primitive Behavior
parallel_for(producer, grain, f: FnMut(Item)) If producer.len() <= grain: run f sequentially over producer.into_iter(). Else let (left,right) = producer.split_at(len/2); join(|| parallel_for(left,grain,f), || parallel_for(right,grain,f)) — recursion reuses Phase 4 join, no extra threads spawned.
parallel_map(producer, grain, f) -> Vec<Output> Same halving; each leaf writes into a pre-allocated output slice (index-stable, no synchronization at leaf); output slice allocated once before recursion and split in parallel with the producer.
parallel_reduce(producer, grain, identity, f: Fn(T,T)->T) -> T Leaf: sequential fold; non-leaf: join left/right subtrees then apply f(left_result, right_result). Associativity required, not commutativity (ordered join). I9: exact match to sequential left-fold for integers; float may reassociate (documented).
parallel_scan(producer, grain, identity, f) -> Vec<T> (inclusive prefix) Upsweep: compute partial reductions per leaf into a reductions: Vec<T> tree. Downsweep: propagate inherited prefix into each leaf's local scan. Both passes reuse parallel_for; O(N) work, O(log N) span.
Adaptive grain StealStats::steals_attempted sampled per split level; steal rate near 0 doubles grain (over-decomposed). AdaptiveGrain { base, current: AtomicUsize } lives in PoolConfig, updated by the pcs-sched heuristic.
SliceProducer<'a, T> Wraps &'a [T]; split_at = slice split_at; zero-copy.
IndexProducer Wraps Range<usize>; produces indices; used for parallel_for over an index space with side effects.

6.3 Deliverable

  • parallel_for, map, reduce, scan compile and produce results matching sequential equivalents.
  • pcs-cli benchmarks: matrix multiply (parallel_for), parallel reduce on 10M integers, scan on 10M elements.
  • Adaptive grain adjusts visibly when OVERDECOMP_FACTOR is set to extreme values.
  • pcs-parallel crate has zero unsafe except where delegated to pcs-deque join internals.

6.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
parallel_for correctness element-wise == sequential over all fixture types
parallel_map correctness output Vec == sequential map, index-stable
parallel_reduce (I9) integer: exact match; float: within 1 ULP or documented
parallel_scan correctness prefix[i] == fold of input[0..=i] for all i
Speedup: reduce 10M i64 ≥ 6× at 8 cores vs sequential
Speedup: matrix multiply 1024² ≥ 5× at 8 cores
Adaptive grain: no over-decomp steal rate < 5% when grain auto-tuned
No races (MIRI / loom) zero data races on slice fixtures

6.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected
pfor_identity.rs parallel_for on 0-length slice no-op, no crash
pfor_single_elem.rs grain > length, no split single sequential run
map_correctness_i32.rs map x*2 on 1M i32 exact Vec match
reduce_sum_i64.rs reduce + on 10M i64 exact sum match

scan_empty.rs scan on empty producer empty Vec
adaptive_grain_stress.rs vary OVERDECOMP_FACTOR 1..1000 no hang, no wrong result
matrix_multiply_bench.rs 1024×1024 f64 matmul matches sequential within 1e-9
