6.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug Signal Recovery
Grain = 0 infinite recursion max(1, …) guard missing stack overflow enforce grain = max(1, grain)
Float reassociation surprise unexpected scan/reduce results parallel tree order differs from sequential I9 fixture mismatch on float document; use integer for conformance tests
Output slice index aliasing in map data corruption split_at and slice split not synchronized wrong result without panic pre-allocate, split output slice mirroring input split
Adaptive grain over-corrects degrades to sequential grain doubles unboundedly speedup collapses cap grain at total / num_threads
scan downsweep prefix wrong incorrect prefix at leaf boundaries off-by-one in inherited prefix accumulation scan fixture mismatch unit test each downsweep level independently
Unsized or non-Send Item compile error Producer::Item not Send compilation failure bound Item: Send on all producer impls

6.7 Performance Concerns

  • LIFO pop for recursive halving: each join pushes the right half; LIFO pop retrieves it immediately = cache-hot (P3); left half runs inline.
  • Output Vec allocation: pre-allocate once; pass mutable sub-slices to leaves; no per-leaf allocation.
  • False sharing in scan upsweep: reductions: Vec<T> laid out sequentially; adjacent reductions may share cache lines if T is small; pad to cache-line size for hot benchmarks.
  • Adaptive grain sampling cost: reading StealStats atomic is a shared read (no contention); sample at every 4th split level to amortize.
  • Over-decomposition ceiling: parallel_for on N=10M with grain=1 spawns ~10M tasks; each task ~50 ns overhead → 500 ms overhead alone; grain must be ≥ 1000 for compute-light bodies.
  • Scan two-pass cost: upsweep + downsweep = 2× memory bandwidth; minimize by fusing scan with a subsequent map if possible.

6.8 Edge Cases

  • Empty producer (len == 0): into_iter() returns empty; reduce returns identity; scan returns empty Vec.
  • Producer length 1: no split; single sequential leaf; no join call.
  • grain > total length: always leaf; fully sequential — correct but not parallel; log at DEBUG level.
  • Non-power-of-two lengths: split_at(len/2) with integer division; right half may be one element larger; correct but asymmetric tree.
  • Nested parallel_for inside another: tasks compete for same worker pool; no deadlock because join does not block; grain must account for nesting depth.
  • reduce with a non-associative operator: no compile-time guard; document invariant; fixture reduce_nonassociative.rs demonstrates mismatch.
  • Very large T (e.g., 1 KiB struct): scan reductions tree = O(N) stack allocation; ensure heap-allocated.
  • parallel_scan with 0 or 1 elements: edge cases in upsweep root; handle explicitly before entering recursion.

6.9 Failure Analysis

  • Data race in parallel_map output slice: two workers write same index; root = split_at mismatch between producer and output slice; detection = MIRI race report; blast radius = silent corruption; recovery = assert left.len() + right.len() == original.len() at every split.
  • Scan incorrect prefix: upsweep partial sums miscalculated; root = off-by-one in tree indexing; detection = fixture scan_prefix_sum.rs mismatch at position > 1; recovery = sequential unit test per tree level.
  • Adaptive grain deadlock: grain set to total length → single-threaded; not a deadlock but a performance cliff; detection = speedup drops to 1×; recovery = cap and alert via pcs-metrics.

6.10 Operational Notes

  • pcs-metrics exposes pfor_splits, pfor_leaves, adaptive_grain_current; monitor pfor_leaves / pfor_splits ratio to detect over-decomposition.
  • pcs-trace emits SplitBegin(depth, len), LeafRun(len) events; flamegraph shows recursion tree shape.
  • OVERDECOMP_FACTOR tunable via PoolConfig; default = 8; increase for compute-heavy tasks, decrease for memory-bandwidth-bound.
  • parallel_scan two-pass is observable as two distinct fan-out levels in the flamegraph.
  • No persistence, no wire format, no migration; in-process only.

Phase 7 — Scheduling Policy + Locality

7.1 Objectives

  • Implement SchedPolicy enum: WorkFirst, HelpFirst, LocalityAware; selectable per PoolConfig.
  • Victim selection strategy: random baseline + NUMA-local bias + optional steal-half.
  • Granularity auto-tuning: adapt leaf-task size threshold so task_cost >> steal_cost.
  • Over-decomposition guard: cap in-flight task count; back-pressure spawn when queue depth exceeds bound.
  • Continuation placement: work-first pushes continuation to deque, runs child directly; help-first reverses.
  • Expose scheduling telemetry via pcs-metrics (steal rates, policy switches, granularity samples).

7.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: P-D (Acar work-first vs help-first empirical comparison); P-F (Cilk-5 work-first proof); P-E (Arora steal analysis, steal-half); B4 Ch.15 (locality-aware scheduling); B5 Ch.27 (granularity auto-tune); ADR-21 (work-first default choice); MM2 (LIFO/FIFO complementarity); MM6 (DFS vs BFS frame footprint); P7; P2; P3 -->

pcs-sched: SchedPolicy + continuation placement

Decision Work-First Help-First
Continuation goes to local deque (pushed by parent) executed by parent thread
Child executed by parent thread (popped next, LIFO) stolen or queued
Benefit deep-first; cache-hot child data breadth-first; expose more parallelism
Risk deep stack depth; deque growth continuation latency; stealing overhead
  • choose_continuation(policy, child_task, cont_task) -> PlacementDecision — signature only; no body.
  • PlacementDecision enum: { RunChild, RunContinuation }.
  • WorkFirst default; HelpFirst switchable per-pool at PoolConfig init; LocalityAware dynamically chooses based on steal pressure reading from pcs-metrics.

Victim selection in pcs-worker

  • Primary: XorShift RNG mod (num_workers); skip self.
  • NUMA-local bias: prefer workers in same NumaNode (from pcs-numa); try k_local attempts before cross-NUMA.
  • k_local: default 3; tunable via PoolConfig.
  • Steal-half option: when a victim deque depth > threshold, steal floor(depth/2) tasks in one pass; requires atomic deque snapshot (approximate; non-blocking).
  • Victim selection loop pseudocode description: pick local-domain victim → attempt steal → on failure, pick random global victim → backoff.

Granularity auto-tuning in pcs-sched

  • Measure steal round-trip cost: time from steal attempt to task-start, sampled per worker.
  • Maintain per-worker GranularityEstimate { steal_cost_ns: u64, last_sample: Instant }.
  • Splitter threshold updated: min_task_ns = GRANULARITY_MULTIPLIER * steal_cost_ns; default multiplier = 10.
  • Exposed as pcs_sched::granularity_threshold() -> Duration for use by pcs-parallel splitters.
  • Update interval: every 1024 steal attempts; use atomic counter.

Over-decomposition control

  • AtomicUsize global in-flight task counter in ThreadPool.
  • spawn_guarded() increments counter; decrements on task Done.
  • If counter > MAX_INFLIGHT = num_threads * OVERDECOMP_FACTOR (default 256), caller does inline execution instead of queuing.
  • MAX_INFLIGHT configurable via PoolConfig.

pcs-metrics instrumentation

  • Per-worker SchedMetrics: fields policy_switches: u64, steal_attempts: u64, steal_wins: u64, gran_threshold_ns: u64.
  • Exposed via pcs_metrics::worker_sched_snapshot(worker_id) -> SchedMetrics.

7.3 Deliverable

  • pcs-sched crate with SchedPolicy, continuation placement, victim selection, granularity tracker.
  • WorkFirst and HelpFirst both operational; LocalityAware selects dynamically based on steal-pressure ratio.
  • Integration test: parallel mergesort under all three policies; verify correct result + measure steal counts.
  • pcs-cli bench: report per-policy speedup, steal rate on 1..N cores.

7.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • WorkFirst speedup >= 0.85 * ideal on mergesort(1M elements) at 8 cores.
  • steal_wins / steal_attempts ratio > 0.3 on compute-heavy workloads (healthy steal yield).
  • Granularity threshold converges within 4096 steal events to within 2x of measured steal cost.
  • Over-decomposition guard: in-flight tasks never exceed num_threads * OVERDECOMP_FACTOR by more than 1 (race window).
  • All three SchedPolicy variants pass correctness fixtures.

7.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected
sched_policy_work_first.toml fork-join tree under WorkFirst result == sequential; deque depth logged
sched_policy_help_first.toml same tree under HelpFirst same result; higher steal count
victim_selection_local_bias.toml 2-NUMA pool, 8 workers/domain cross-NUMA steals < 20% of total
granularity_convergence.toml 10000-task micro-job flood gran_threshold stabilizes
overdecomp_guard.toml spawn loop exceeding MAX_INFLIGHT inline fallback; no OOM
steal_half.toml imbalanced load: 1 worker deep, rest idle steal-half drains within 2 rounds

7.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug Signal
Work-first deep stack stack overflow in recursive divide-and-conquer continuation pushed unboundedly; each frame retained stack_size exhaustion; backtrace depth > 1000
Help-first latency cliff 2x slowdown vs work-first on cache-hot workloads continuation crossed to different worker; cold cache cache miss counters (perf); steal_wins spike
Granularity estimator lag thrashing fine-grained tasks after burst steal_cost sample taken during cold period gran_threshold_ns drops to single-digit ns
Victim selection livelock idle workers repeatedly pick same empty victim RNG seed collision or small worker count steal_attempts >> steal_wins for specific worker_id
Over-decomposition bypass race in-flight count races near MAX_INFLIGHT non-atomic read-then-branch counter momentarily exceeds bound by thread-count
Steal-half ABA on snapshot duplicate task execution non-atomic depth snapshot + concurrent pops I3 violation; double Done on TaskState

7.7 Performance Concerns

  • Victim RNG: XorShift is 1 ns; avoid thread_rng (mutex); seeded from worker_id XOR timestamp.
  • NUMA-local loop: k_local = 3 adds at most 3 failed CAS round-trips before cross-NUMA; acceptable.
  • Granularity estimator: Instant::now() per steal is ~20 ns; sample only 1-in-N (N=64) to amortize.
  • Steal-half: requires atomic load of deque bottom; approximate (may undercount); acceptable staleness.
  • SchedPolicy branch: single match on enum; branch predictor stabilizes after warmup.
  • Over-decomposition counter: single AtomicUsize fetch_add; ~5 ns; on hot spawn path, use relaxed ordering with periodic fence.

7.8 Edge Cases

  • Single worker: victim loop finds no valid target; must not spin; immediately park.
  • All workers same NUMA node: NUMA-local bias degenerates to random; no performance regression.
  • steal_cost_ns == 0: measured during profiling disabled build; clamp to 1 ns to avoid divide-by-zero in threshold calc.
  • MAX_INFLIGHT == 0 in PoolConfig: treat as unlimited (disable guard); documented behavior.
  • Policy switch mid-execution: PoolConfig policy is read once at worker init; no runtime switch; document.
  • LocalityAware with no metrics yet: default to WorkFirst until first steal sample collected.

7.9 Failure Analysis

  • Incorrect granularity threshold: tasks too fine → steal overhead dominates → throughput drops 5-10x. Detection: steal_wins / tasks_run ratio < 0.01. Recovery: manual override via PoolConfig.min_task_ns.
  • Victim selection starvation: one worker never chosen as victim due to RNG bias. Detection: per-worker steal_attempts distribution skewed. Containment: add round-robin fallback every 16 attempts.
  • Steal-half double-execution (I1 violation): data corruption in reduction result. Detection: conformance fixture mismatch. Blast radius: silent wrong answer. Recovery: disable steal-half, file deque bug.

7.10 Operational Notes

  • pcs_metrics::sched_report() emits per-worker JSON: policy, steal_rate, gran_threshold, overdecomp_rejections.
  • PoolConfig.policy logged at pool init (tracing::info).
  • Granularity threshold exported as gauge metric pcs.sched.gran_threshold_ns[worker_id].
  • Steal-half enabled via PoolConfig.steal_half = true; off by default; flag in metrics output.
  • Policy tuning guide: use WorkFirst for recursive divide-and-conquer; HelpFirst for producer-consumer pipelines; LocalityAware for mixed workloads.

Phase 8 — Futures / Promises + Continuations

8.1 Objectives

  • Implement Future<T> / Promise<T> pair in pcs-sync: task result handle without thread blocking.
  • Future::wait() participates in the scheduler (runs other tasks) rather than blocking the OS thread.
  • Continuation tasks chained on Future completion: .then(|val| ...) spawns a dependent task.
  • Express dependency as future readiness: a task node in pcs-graph becomes Ready when its input Future is fulfilled.
  • Integrate with Phase 5 DAG: fulfilled Promise decrements successor pending_deps.
  • Panic from producing task captured in Promise; re-raised at Future::wait() site.

8.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: P-M (async futures poll model); B2 Ch.21 (Waker + Poll<T> design); B9 Ch.25 (condvar-free futex wait); S8 (UnwindSafe for panic-safe promises); ADR-22 (Future<T> vs async fn); ADR-23 (continuation chain implementation); MM9 (park/unpark level-triggered); P8 -->

pcs-sync: Future<T> / Promise<T> types

// Signatures only — no bodies
struct Promise<T: Send> { inner: Arc<FutureInner<T>> }
struct Future<T: Send>  { inner: Arc<FutureInner<T>> }

struct FutureInner<T: Send> {
    state: AtomicU8,              // FutureState ordinal
    value: UnsafeCell<MaybeUninit<T>>,
    panic_payload: UnsafeCell<Option<Box<dyn Any + Send>>>,
    continuations: Mutex<Vec<Task>>,
    latch: Latch,
}

enum FutureState { Pending = 0, Fulfilled = 1, Panicked = 2 }

impl<T: Send> Promise<T> {
    fn fulfill(self, val: T);           // stores val, transitions state, fires continuations
    fn panic(self, payload: Box<dyn Any + Send>);
}

impl<T: Send> Future<T> {
    fn wait(self) -> T;                 // contract: runs scheduler tasks until Fulfilled; re-raises on Panicked
    fn then<U, F>(self, f: F) -> Future<U>   // spawns continuation task; returns new Future<U>
        where F: FnOnce(T) -> U + Send + 'static, U: Send;
    fn is_ready(&self) -> bool;
}

Mechanism table (module layout — future.rs/latch.rs/barrier.rs/lib.rs re-exports — matches §10 Fragment C pcs-sync/):

Mechanism Behavior
Scheduler-participatory wait (pcs-worker) Future::wait() never calls thread::park/condvar.wait directly; it loops { if inner.latch.is_set() { return take_value() } run_one_task_from_local_or_steal() } — the same pop-local/steal logic as the main worker loop, keeping the waiting thread productive; after 65536 iterations it issues one OS sched yield, then parks on the latch.
Continuation chaining then() creates a new (Promise<U>, Future<U>) pair, wraps f as a Task capturing the input value, and registers it in FutureInner.continuations before checking state; if already Fulfilled at registration it is pushed to the caller's local deque immediately. Promise::fulfill() atomically transitions state then drains continuations into the current worker's deque (or Injector with no worker context). Chain depth is unlimited — each .then() is one extra task.
DAG integration (pcs-graph) A GraphNode may hold a Future<()> input edge instead of a static Dependency; on fulfillment it calls graph_node_ready_check(node_id), which decrements pending_deps and pushes to the deque at zero (Phase 5 mechanism). future_dependency(future, node_id) registers the linkage in FutureInner.continuations.
Latch internals Latch = AtomicBool + condvar fallback. set(): Release store true, wakes parked waiters. is_set(): Acquire load. wait_park(): parks on the condvar, used only once the 65536-iteration spin budget in Future::wait() is exhausted.

8.3 Deliverable

  • pcs-sync crate: Future/Promise, Latch, Barrier, WaitGroup compile and pass unit tests.
  • future::wait() demonstrated not to block OS thread: a pool of N workers all waiting on futures continues to make progress (no deadlock).
  • .then() chains of depth 10 execute correctly; panic propagation through chain verified.
  • Integration with pcs-graph: future-gated DAG node activated on fulfillment.

8.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • No OS thread blocking in Future::wait() under normal fulfillment path (verified via thread-count assertion: parked threads == 0 during active compute).
  • .then() chain of 1000 tasks completes with correct final value vs sequential computation.
  • Panic in producing task: Future::wait() re-raises the same panic payload (type matches).
  • Continuation registered after fulfillment executes immediately (within 1 scheduler loop iteration).
  • future_dependency(future, node_id) correctly activates DAG node: conformance fixture passes.
  • Zero memory leaks after Future/Promise drop with no wait() called (Arc drops clean; Miri clean).

8.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected
future_basic_fulfill.toml promise fulfilled before wait value received, no park
future_wait_before_fulfill.toml wait called before fulfill wait runs tasks; eventually receives value
future_then_chain.toml 10-deep .then() chain final value == sequential fold
future_panic_propagation.toml producer panics wait() re-raises; downstream .then() not called
future_dag_gate.toml DAG node gated on future node activates exactly once on fulfill
future_drop_no_wait.toml Future dropped without wait no leak; Promise::fulfill does not panic
future_concurrent_then.toml 8 threads call .then() concurrently all continuations execute exactly once

8.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug Signal
Wait spins forever one worker busy-loops in wait() producing task never queued / lost steal_attempts spike; progress counter stalls
Continuation lost .then() task never runs race: state check before registration; task dropped future_continuation_registered counter == N but tasks_run < N
Double-drop of value UB on second wait() Future::wait() takes ownership but inner still has value Miri: use-after-move; add taken: AtomicBool guard
Panic payload type mismatch wrong panic on re-raise payload downcast fails silently add assert on downcast success in test
Continuation chain deadlock all workers waiting on each other's futures circular future dependency cycle detection in DAG integration; graph fixture
Memory growth in long chains Arc never drops continuation holds Future<U> which holds Arc<FutureInner<U>> heap profiler; chain drop test

8.7 Performance Concerns

  • continuations: Mutex<Vec<Task>> is on critical path of fulfill(); use a lock-free push-list or short-circuit when no continuations registered (common case).
  • is_set() hot loop in wait(): Acquire load on cacheline shared with Promise's Release store; place state atomic on separate cacheline from value.
  • Then-chain allocation: each .then() allocates Arc + Task box; for 1000-deep chains, pre-size Vec or use a slab.
  • fulfill() draining continuations into Injector (no current worker): Injector is MPMC; one push per continuation; acceptable for shallow chains; batch-push if len > 8.
  • Latch condvar park: only after 65536 iterations; amortizes condvar overhead over compute-heavy wait.

8.8 Edge Cases

  • Future::wait() called from outside thread pool (main thread): no local deque; can only steal from Injector; must degrade gracefully (run injector tasks or park on latch without spinning).
  • Multiple callers of wait() on same Future (cloned): each spins independently; only one takes value (UnsafeCell guarded by AtomicBool).
  • Zero-sized T: layout is fine with MaybeUninit<()>; fulfillment just sets state.
  • Future fulfilled with value of size > cache line: value write and state transition must not be reordered (Release fence after value store, before state CAS).
  • .then() after panic state: must not execute continuation; document as fire-only-on-success variant.
  • Pool shutdown while futures pending: treat as Panicked with ShutdownError payload; Future::wait() returns Err or panics.

8.9 Failure Analysis

  • Thread starvation deadlock: all N workers blocked in Future::wait() on futures that require new tasks to be spawned, but no thread can spawn. Detection: idle counter == N for > 1 s. Containment: compensation thread (Phase 12) injector-pumps tasks. Recovery: structured cancellation of waiting futures.
  • Continuation double-execution: I1 violation; wrong reduction results. Detection: TaskState transitions Pending→Running twice (assert). Blast radius: data corruption. Recovery: rerun with determinism mode (Phase 14).
  • Lost continuation: future fulfilled, continuation task silently dropped. Detection: WaitGroup count mismatch in fixtures. Recovery: add Arc-strong-count assertion after chain completion.

8.10 Operational Notes

  • pcs_metrics counters: future_fulfillments_total, future_continuations_fired, future_waits_parked, future_waits_spinning.
  • pcs-trace: FutureState transitions emitted as span events with TaskId of producing + consuming tasks.
  • Future::wait() path logged at TRACE level with worker_id; aids deadlock postmortem.
  • Pool shutdown procedure must fulfill or panic all pending Futures before thread join (I10).
  • Async-Rust interop: not in scope; Future<T> is a synchronous task-future, not std::future::Future.

Phase 9 — NUMA-Aware Scheduling

9.1 Objectives

  • Detect NUMA topology at pool init: domains, per-domain CPU sets, memory nodes.
  • Group workers by NumaNode; assign each worker a domain at construction.
  • Per-domain deque groups: within-domain steal attempted before cross-domain.
  • Allocate task data on the running domain's memory node when feasible.
  • Per-domain Injectors: external submissions hashed to a domain; cross-domain inject as fallback.
  • Remote-steal (cross-NUMA) as last resort: bounded attempts before parking.
  • Expose per-domain metrics: within-domain steal rate, cross-domain steal rate, domain imbalance.

9.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: P-K (Drebes topology-aware work-stealing, NUMA groups); S3 (hwloc topology API); S4 (libnuma numactl); B5 Ch.12 (NUMA cost model, memory latency table); ADR-11 (cross-domain steal gate); ADR-10 (CpuSet binding); MM3 (bandwidth saturation at NUMA boundary, Back-of-Envelope Capacity Model step 5); P9 -->

pcs-numa: topology detection

// Signatures only
struct NumaTopology { domains: Vec<NumaDomain> }
struct NumaDomain   { id: u32, cpus: CpuSet, workers: Vec<WorkerId>, injector: Injector<Task> }

fn detect_numa_topology() -> NumaTopology;   // reads /sys/devices/system/node or hwloc bindings
fn bind_thread_to_domain(domain_id: u32) -> Result<(), AffinityError>;
fn alloc_on_node<T>(domain_id: u32, f: impl FnOnce() -> T) -> T;  // hint; best-effort via mbind
  • Topology detection parses /sys/devices/system/node/node*/cpulist on Linux (fallback to single-domain on unsupported OS); hwloc is an optional feature flag — if disabled, use the sysfs parser only.
  • CpuSet: newtype over [u64; 16] bitmask (supports up to 1024 CPUs).
  • Workers assigned to domains round-robin if num_threads > domain CPU count.

Steal order in worker loop

1. Pop own deque (LIFO)
2. Attempt k_local steals from local_stealers (random among same-domain workers)
3. Check own domain's Injector
4. Attempt k_remote steals from remote_stealers (random among cross-domain workers)
5. Check global Injector
6. Exponential backoff → park
  • k_local default 4; k_remote default 2; both configurable in PoolConfig. Step 3 (domain Injector) is drained before cross-domain steal so local queued work goes first.

Worker init, injectors, allocation, and metrics:

Mechanism Behavior
Worker init (pcs-worker) Each Worker stores numa_domain: u32; its Stealer list splits into local_stealers: Vec<Stealer> (same domain) and remote_stealers: Vec<Stealer> (other domains); thread affinity is set via bind_thread_to_domain() at worker-thread start.
Per-domain Injectors ThreadPool holds Vec<Injector<Task>> indexed by domain_id. pool.submit_with_affinity(task, domain_hint) pushes to injectors[domain_hint]; plain pool.submit(task) hashes task.id % num_domains for deterministic placement. Workers in domain D drain injectors[D] before stealing cross-domain.
NUMA-local data allocation alloc_on_node uses libc::mbind with MPOL_BIND on the allocated region (no-op on non-Linux). Parallel arrays (parallel_for) are pre-partitioned per domain — domain D owns [D*chunk_size .. (D+1)*chunk_size]; the splitter treats domain boundaries as a split preference, not a hard constraint.
pcs-numa metrics Per-domain NumaMetrics { within_steal: u64, cross_steal: u64, injector_drained: u64 }; domain imbalance = max(tasks_run[d]) / min(tasks_run[d]), target < 1.3.

9.3 Deliverable

  • pcs-numa crate: topology detection, domain binding, per-domain injectors operational on Linux.
  • Worker loop updated with domain-aware steal order.
  • Integration test on 2-NUMA machine (or emulated via numactl --hardware in CI): cross-domain steal rate < 20% on NUMA-local workload.
  • pcs-cli benchmark: matrix multiply partitioned by domain; report per-domain task throughput.

9.4 Acceptance Criteria

  • Topology detection produces correct domain count on test machine (verified against numactl --hardware).
  • Within-domain steal fraction >= 0.80 on embarrassingly parallel workload with NUMA-local data.
  • Cross-domain steals < 20% of total steals on workloads where data is domain-partitioned.
  • Domain imbalance ratio < 1.3 on balanced workloads (no pathological skew).
  • Single-domain fallback: all tests pass on non-NUMA hardware with no regression.
  • Thread affinity: pthread_getaffinity_np confirms each worker thread bound to correct CpuSet.

9.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected
numa_topology_linux.toml parse sysfs on 2-domain machine domain count == 2; correct CPU sets
numa_single_domain_fallback.toml non-NUMA host single domain; all workers in domain 0
numa_local_steal_bias.toml 8 workers, 2 domains, local load within_steal / total_steal >= 0.80
numa_cross_domain_steal.toml one domain idle, other overloaded cross-domain steals activate; imbalance resolved
numa_injector_hash.toml 100 tasks submitted with domain hints each domain's injector receives expected count
numa_affinity_binding.toml read back cpu affinity after init pthread affinity matches CpuSet
numa_data_partition.toml parallel_for on NUMA-partitioned array cross-domain memory accesses < 10% of total

9.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root Cause Debug Signal
Topology parse failure all workers in domain 0 sysfs path absent or permission denied warn log at init; single_domain_fallback metric
Affinity binding ignored workers run on wrong CPUs sched_setaffinity EPERM in container AffinityError logged; cross-domain steal rate spikes
Cross-domain steal storm latency spikes on NUMA-remote tasks k_remote too high; domain injector not drained first cross_steal counter spike; add domain-injector drain before cross steal
mbind allocation failure task data not on local node process lacks CAP_SYS_NICE or mbind unsupported log ENOSYS; disable alloc_on_node; no correctness impact
Domain injector starvation tasks piling up in domain D injector workers in D all idle-parked while another domain's workers are busy domain_injector_depth gauge; unpark domain-D workers on inject
Worker domain assignment skew one domain has 2x workers round-robin assignment with unequal CPU counts domain_worker_count metric; adjust assignment

9.7 Performance Concerns

  • NUMA remote access latency: ~1.5–2x vs local on typical 2-socket x86; partitioning data by domain is the primary optimization.
  • Per-domain Injector: separate cacheline per domain; avoids false sharing between domain 0 and domain 1 injectors.
  • bind_thread_to_domain: one sched_setaffinity syscall at thread start, negligible; mbind is a per-allocation syscall — use only for large arrays (>= 1 MB), skip for per-task stack data.
  • k_local steal loop (4 attempts): 4 × CAS round-trips ~= 4 × 30 ns = 120 ns; acceptable vs task execution cost.
  • Cross-domain steal adds ~100 ns extra cache coherency cost; bounded by k_remote = 2 attempts before park.
  • Domain imbalance detection: ratio computed lazily on metrics snapshot; not on hot path.

9.8 Edge Cases

  • Single-core machine: 1 domain, 1 worker, no stealing; all NUMA code degenerates gracefully.
  • Worker count < num_domains: some domains have 0 workers; their Injectors never drained; detect and warn; route submissions to populated domains.
  • Container / VM with restricted topology: sysfs may report incorrect NUMA; add PoolConfig.force_domains: Option<u32> override.
  • Hot-add CPU (live topology change): not supported; document that topology is frozen at pool init.
  • NUMA domain with heterogeneous cores (performance + efficiency): CpuSet includes all; no scheduling distinction; future extension.
  • numactl --membind wrapper restricting memory: mbind may fail silently; measure via numastat; log warning.

9.9 Failure Analysis

  • Total affinity failure (container): all workers on same physical core → serialized execution → throughput = 1 core. Detection: pool_effective_parallelism gauge = 1.0. Containment: log AffinityError; continue without affinity. Recovery: run outside container or with --cap-add SYS_NICE.
  • Domain injector never drained: tasks accumulate → OOM. Detection: domain_injector_depth gauge monotonically increasing. Blast radius: memory exhaustion. Containment: unpark workers in target domain; add backpressure at submit boundary (P10).
  • Topology parse wrong domain count: workers over-distributed or under-distributed → severe imbalance. Detection: imbalance ratio > 2.0 at steady state. Recovery: PoolConfig.force_domains override; restart pool.

9.10 Operational Notes

  • pcs_metrics::numa_report() emits per-domain JSON: workers, within_steal, cross_steal, injector_depth, tasks_run.
  • Topology summary logged at INFO on pool start: numa_domains=2 workers_per_domain=[4,4].
  • domain_imbalance_ratio exported as gauge; alert threshold > 1.5 indicates workload or data partitioning issue.
  • pcs-trace annotates each task span with numa_domain tag; enables per-domain flamegraph breakdown.
  • NUMA metrics should be checked after any workload change; cross-domain steal rate > 30% indicates data layout needs re-partitioning.
  • On single-domain hosts (most CI), NUMA code path is exercised via single-domain fallback; multi-NUMA integration tests require hardware or numactl --hardware emulation.

Phase 10 — Priority + Fairness + Backpressure

10.1 Objectives

  • Add optional priority lanes to pcs-pool and pcs-sched without breaking the common single-priority path.
  • Implement a bounded global Injector<Task> for external submissions; apply backpressure (block caller or return Err) when in-flight count reaches a configurable ceiling.
  • Guarantee no worker starves: any lower-priority task must make progress within a bounded number of high-priority task completions (fairness_quanta).
  • Expose submit() / try_submit() / submit_batch() / submit_from_task() public APIs with explicit backpressure semantics.
  • Constraints: priority logic adds zero synchronization to the hot steal path when lanes == 1; fairness must be verified by automated test, not just by design intent; I6 must hold (no oversubscription from priority lanes).
  • Phase scope: pcs-pool, pcs-sched, pcs-metrics; no changes to pcs-deque, pcs-worker internals, or pcs-graph.
  • Phase dependencies: Phase 1 (thread pool), Phase 2 (deque), Phase 3 (task model + spawn) must be complete.

Priority lane invariant (additional): a task submitted to lane N executes before a task in lane M only if N > M, unless FairnessGuard forces a lower-priority task through — no silent priority promotion/demotion. spawn() (fork-join, pcs-parallel) always uses the highest-priority lane to preserve work-first semantics (Phase 7); only external submit() uses the caller-specified lane.

10.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: P-J (Michael-Scott queue for per-priority injectors); B4 Ch.16 (priority scheduling theory); B9 Ch.26 (bounded queues, backpressure); ADR-15 (priority inversion mitigation); ADR-06 (Injector bounded design); P10; I6 -->

Mechanism table (pcs-pool, pcs-sched):

Mechanism Behavior
Priority lane data structures PoolConfig gains priority_lanes: u8 (default 1, max 8); SchedPolicy gains PriorityAware { lanes: u8 }. Worker holds local_lanes: SmallVec<[ChaseLevDeque<Task>; 4]>, push/pop selecting the highest non-empty lane; cfg!(feature = "single-priority") collapses this to one deque at compile time, eliminating the lane-loop branch. ThreadPool holds injectors: Box<[Injector<Task>]> sized to priority_lanes; external submit(task, lane) pushes to injectors[lane]. Steal loop iterates a victim's lanes high-to-low, preserving P1/P3 while respecting priority. spawn_with_priority(task, lane: u8) sets lane at spawn; default spawn uses lane = priority_lanes - 1 (highest) to avoid breaking existing fork-join usage. Task gains a priority: u8 field for metrics attribution (tasks_run_total{lane=N}) and FairnessGuard streak tracking.
In-flight bounding PoolConfig gains max_in_flight: usize (0 = unlimited); ThreadPool maintains in_flight: AtomicUsize. submit(task, lane): fetch_add(1, Acquire) on in_flight; result >= max_in_flight parks the caller on a Condvar until a slot frees, then pushes to injectors[lane]. try_submit(...) -> Result<TaskId, BackpressureError> is non-blocking: compare_exchange only if < max_in_flight, else immediate Err(Saturated). submit_batch(tasks, lane) increments in_flight by tasks.len() in one fetch_add, returning Err(BatchExceedsCapacity { available }) with no partial submission on insufficient capacity. On task completion, fetch_sub(1, Release); if prev > max_in_flight / 2, condvar.notify_one() (hysteresis: notify only when crossing the midpoint, to avoid a notify-storm).
Fairness guard Worker gains thread-local hp_run_streak: u32 (no atomic needed), incremented after each completion of a priority > 0 task. When hp_run_streak >= config.fairness_quanta (default 64), the worker force-pops one task from the lowest non-empty lane before resuming high-priority work, then resets the streak. A background pcs-metrics starvation detector computes lane_idle_ns{lane=N} per interval (idle time attributable to a non-empty-but-unexecuted lane) and emits WARN StarvationDetected { lane, idle_ns } past threshold. fairness_quanta and starvation_threshold_ns are PoolConfig fields, construction-time only.
External submission path Workers drain injectors[lane] in the idle loop after exhausting local deques, stealing up to 32 tasks per drain cycle to amortize MPMC CAS overhead; drain order matches lane index (N before N-1). submit from within a running task is allowed but must use the submit_from_task() variant, which skips the in_flight increment (already counted) and pushes directly to the calling worker's local deque via CURRENT_WORKER, bypassing the Injector for lower overhead. TaskId generation uses an AtomicU64 fetch_add(1, Relaxed) in ThreadPool, returned from submit()/submit_batch() for tracing.

10.3 Deliverable

  • ThreadPool::submit(), try_submit(), submit_batch(), submit_from_task() compile and pass correctness tests.
  • Priority lane separation verified by ordering test: high-priority tasks complete before low-priority tasks queued simultaneously on a pool with no competing load.
  • Backpressure test: producer blocks then resumes correctly when slots free; try_submit returns Err with zero parking.
  • Fairness test: low-priority tasks complete within 2 * fairness_quanta high-priority completions.
  • pcs-metrics exports lane_depth{lane=N}, starvation_idle_ns{lane=N}, tasks_run_total{lane=N} gauges.
  • TaskId returned from all submit variants; traceable in pcs-trace spans.
  • spawn_with_priority API available in pcs-parallel for user-controlled priority on fork-join spawns.
  • PoolConfig::priority_lanes validated at construction: 1 ≤ lanes ≤ 8; returns Err(ConfigError::InvalidLanes) otherwise.
  • All single-priority pools (lanes = 1) pass existing Phase 1–9 benchmarks without regression; criterion confirms.
  • Starvation detector background task initializes with pool; stops cleanly at pool shutdown; no orphaned metric threads.

10.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Threshold / Mechanism
test_priority_ordering 100 hp + 100 lp tasks; hp completes first in > 99% of 10 000 runs
test_backpressure_blocks max_in_flight=8; 16 concurrent submits; 8 block, unblock on completion
test_try_submit_err returns Err(BackpressureError::Saturated) with zero parking
test_fairness_no_starvation 1000 hp + 10 lp tasks; lp completes; no lp task waits > 2*fairness_quanta hp tasks
test_submit_batch_atomic submit_batch on saturated pool: no partial submission (0 or all)
Single-lane overhead criterion: < 2 ns/task vs baseline (single-priority feature flag)
in_flight counter accuracy matches task count ±1 across 1 M concurrent submits (loom model check)
test_lane_drain_order injector with 3 lanes; worker drains highest first

10.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture Purpose Expected output
priority_ordering.txt 100 hp + 100 lp tasks, interleaved submit hp tasks all appear before lp in completion log

try_submit_nblock.txt max_in_flight=1, saturated pool second try_submit returns Err(Saturated)
fairness_streak.txt 1000 hp + 10 lp tasks lp tasks complete; no lp task waits > 2*fairness_quanta hp completions
single_lane_perf.txt criterion micro with feature single-priority throughput ≥ baseline ± 2%
malformed_priority.txt priority >= lanes passed to submit returns Err(InvalidPriority { given, max }), no panic
submit_batch_partial.txt submit_batch on pool with available=3, batch size 10 returns Err(BatchExceedsCapacity { available: 3 }); 0 tasks enqueued

10.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug signal Recovery
in_flight double-decrement counter goes negative; OOM or wrong backpressure compensation path also decrements on cancel fetch_sub result underflows (check result == 0 before returning to pool) assert prev > 0 in decrement; metrics underflow alert
Starvation despite FairnessGuard lp tasks never run all workers have non-zero hp streak but guard checks per-worker, not globally; pool saturated with hp tasks faster than quanta resets lane_idle_ns{lane=0} growing; starvation detector fires lower fairness_quanta; operator alert
Lock convoy on backpressure Condvar submitters pile up; wakeup storm notify_one under-notifies burst completions park_count spikes in submit metrics use hysteresis: notify only when in_flight crosses max_in_flight / 2 downward
Priority inversion in steal thief steals lp from victim while hp exists on own deque steal loop does not consult own queues first hp lane depth non-zero on idle worker while lp lane depleted elsewhere fix steal priority: exhaust own hp lanes before stealing any lp work from others
submit_batch partial increment crash mid-batch leaves phantom in_flight slots batch fetch_add succeeds but some tasks fail to enqueue in_flight > actual queue depth increment per-task inside batch loop; compensate (decrement) on enqueue failure
notify_one under backpressure one submitter woken, re-blocks, others not woken burst of completions with only one notify_one per completion blocked-submitters gauge stuck > 0 on completion, notify count = min(completions_this_batch, blocked_submitters)

10.7 Performance Concerns

  • Multi-lane pop loop: branch prediction miss if lanes is runtime-dynamic; const generics or cfg feature flag collapses to zero overhead for single-priority pools.
  • Condvar under backpressure: do not hold any deque lock across condvar.notify; release all locks before notification to avoid priority inversion on the mutex.
  • in_flight AtomicUsize: one fetch_add per submit, one fetch_sub per task completion; shared across all workers; submit_batch amortizes to one fetch_add per batch; on a 64-core machine with 64 concurrent submissions this is the bottleneck — measure and tune.
  • Injector drain: bulk-steal 32 tasks per drain cycle; single CAS per task in crossbeam injector; batching reduces CAS frequency by 32×.
  • fairness_quanta check: one u32 increment per task completion (thread-local, no atomic); branch is nearly always not-taken; branch predictor holds.
  • Lane iteration in steal: bounded by lanes (≤ 8 by design); unrolled by compiler at lanes = 2–4.
  • submit_from_task() bypasses Injector and in_flight check entirely; approximately 3–5 ns vs 30–50 ns for external submit(); prefer this form for recursive task spawning inside pcs-parallel.
  • SmallVec<[ChaseLevDeque; 4]>: stack-allocated for ≤ 4 lanes; heap-allocated only for 5–8 lanes; cache-hot for common 1–2 lane configurations.

10.8 Edge Cases

  • max_in_flight = 0: treated as unlimited; bypass the in_flight check entirely; no Condvar allocated.
  • fairness_quanta = 0: every task is round-robin across all lanes regardless of priority; valid configuration; document.
  • Pool shutdown with blocked submitters: shutdown() wakes all blocked submit() callers with Err(ShutdownError); in_flight counter not required to reach 0 during abort shutdown.
  • submit called from a worker thread: must use submit_from_task() to avoid double-counting; calling submit() from inside a task increments in_flight (incorrect); document and add debug assertion checking CURRENT_WORKER.is_none().
  • priority_lanes = 1 at runtime (not feature flag): lane loop executes once; correct but slightly slower than feature-flag path; document perf implication.
  • submit_batch with empty iterator: no-op; returns Ok(empty vec); no in_flight increment.
  • Lane u8 overflow: lane >= priority_lanes passed to submit(); returns Err(InvalidPriority) without enqueuing; never panics.
  • Worker with no local tasks and empty injectors parks on Condvar / futex; submit() must unpark at least one idle worker after pushing to injector; use ThreadPool::unpark_one() after successful injector push.
  • starvation_threshold_ns = 0: starvation detector disabled; no background metric computation; use u64::MAX equivalent sentinel.
  • Backpressure Condvar spurious wakeups: re-check in_flight < max_in_flight after wakeup before proceeding; loop in submit().

10.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Containment Recovery
Backpressure deadlock (all workers blocked in submit) in_flight == num_threads AND all workers parked; watchdog timeout all compute frozen try_submit with timeout at call site; do not submit from within a task; use submit_from_task() variant inject canary task from an OS thread outside the pool; lower max_in_flight
Starvation of low-priority lane lane_idle_ns{lane=0} exceeds threshold lp work accumulates unboundedly; OOM risk if lp queue unbounded starvation detector emits WARN; reduces fairness_quanta temporarily operator tunes fairness_quanta down; rebalances task mix; set max_in_flight per-lane if needed
Injector overflow (unbounded queue growth) heap memory grows; injector_len gauge spikes; OOM warning from allocator OOM kill max_in_flight ceiling prevents unbounded growth if configured enforce non-zero max_in_flight in all production deployments; emit BackpressureError at boundary
Double notification storm thousands of wakeups/s post-saturation; park_count spike CPU burn in Condvar internals; latency spikes park/unpark rate gauge alert apply hysteresis on notification (notify only at in_flight midpoint crossing); batch wake
in_flight underflow (counter goes negative) fetch_sub result underflows usize; wraps to usize::MAX backpressure permanently disabled; unlimited task submission in_flight > max_in_flight invariant violation alert debug assert prev > 0 in decrement; saturating subtract in release mode as safety net
Lane u8 index truncation tasks silently routed to wrong lane caller passes lane as usize and truncates to u8 with loss unexpected lane_depth distribution in metrics validate lane index as u8 in public API; return Err(InvalidPriority) for out-of-range

Cross-phase interactions: Phase 3 (task model) — Task.priority: u8 added, non-breaking (default u8::MAX/highest if absent); Phase 5 (DAG) — nodes submit via submit_from_task() on lane 0 by default, overridable via GraphNode { lane }; Phase 7 (sched policy) — PriorityAware variant of SchedPolicy overrides WorkFirst/HelpFirst steal ordering with the lane-aware loop; Phase 9 (NUMA) — per-domain injectors drain domain-local before cross-domain, with pcs-numa providing routing that pcs-pool consumes; Phase 12 (blocking) — submit_from_task() inside block_in_place is allowed and skips in_flight, and compensation threads use the standard steal loop regardless of lane; Phase 13 (observability) — lane_depth/tasks_run_total{lane} are exported by pcs-metrics and trace spans carry a task.lane attribute.

10.10 Operational Notes

  • pcs-metrics exports: lane_depth{lane=N}, in_flight, backpressure_wait_ns histogram, starvation_idle_ns{lane=N}, tasks_run_total{lane=N}, backpressure_rejected_total, submit_batch_calls_total, submit_from_task_calls_total.
  • starvation_threshold_ns and fairness_quanta are construction-time PoolConfig fields; no runtime mutation, and neither does priority_lanes/max_in_flight — changing either requires a pool rebuild.
  • try_submit preferred for latency-sensitive producers (no parking); submit for throughput pipelines; submit_batch for bulk producer patterns; submit_from_task for recursive spawning inside tasks.
  • Backpressure events logged at WARN level with caller task ID, lane, and current in_flight value.
  • Starvation detector runs as a periodic metric aggregation (default interval: 1 s); does not add per-task overhead.
  • lane_depth gauge updated on every push/pop in worker loop; sampled by metrics collector at configurable interval; acceptable staleness ≤ 1 collection interval.
  • Debugging priority misconfiguration: compare tasks_run_total{lane=N} ratios against submitted ratios; imbalance beyond fairness_quanta indicates misconfiguration.
  • max_in_flight tuning guideline: start with num_threads * 4; increase if backpressure_wait_ns p99 > latency SLA; decrease if heap grows unboundedly.

Phase 11 — Cancellation + Panic Propagation

11.1 Objectives

  • Implement structured cancellation: a CancellationToken that long-running tasks poll; cancellation propagates hierarchically through a Scope tree. Implement panic propagation: any panicking child task poisons its owning Scope, re-raising at the scope.wait()/join() boundary with the original payload.
  • Structured shutdown: ThreadPool::shutdown(mode) drains in-flight tasks (DrainThenStop) or aborts them cleanly (AbortInFlight); no detached tasks remain after shutdown, and (I10 extended) every worker thread — including Phase 12 compensation threads — is joined before in_flight == 0 is asserted. Constraints: CancellationToken::is_cancelled() is zero-cost when not cancelled (single Relaxed load); panic propagation must not silently swallow payloads; invariants I7, I8, I10 must hold. A Cancelled task contributes to scope completion exactly as a Done task does — the scope does not re-raise on cancellation alone; a task that begins executing after cancel() transitions to Cancelled immediately (pre-check), while an already-executing task must check is_cancelled() at yield points to stop cooperatively.
  • Phase scope: pcs-sync (CancellationToken, PanicCell, Latch), pcs-pool (shutdown), pcs-worker (catch_unwind), pcs-parallel (Scope lifetime safety). Dependencies: Phase 4 (fork-join Scope), Phase 3 (TaskState), Phase 5 (DAG) must be complete.

11.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: S8 (catch_unwind + UnwindSafe for scope poisoning); B2 Ch.19 (Scope<'s> invariant lifetime + poison flag); ADR-24 (CancellationToken cooperative vs preemptive); ADR-25 (scope drain vs abort on cancel); I7 (scope not-return-early); I8 (panic propagation contract); MM6 (structured concurrency definition) -->

CancellationToken — pcs-sync:

struct CancellationToken { cancelled: Arc<AtomicBool> }
impl CancellationToken {
    fn new() -> Self;
    fn cancel(&self);                      // SeqCst store; idempotent
    fn is_cancelled(&self) -> bool;        // Relaxed load; zero-cost when not cancelled
    fn child(&self) -> CancellationToken;  // clones Arc; shares flag
}

  • Task gains cancel_token: Option<CancellationToken>; the worker checks token.is_cancelled() (Relaxed load) before invoking the task body and again at yield_to_scheduler() call sites; on detection it transitions TaskState → Cancelled (new terminal state after Done) — cancelled tasks still decrement in_flight and scope completion counters so Scope::wait() returns normally (no panic) when only cancellation occurred.

  • DAG integration (pcs-graph): a cancelled GraphNode still decrements successors' pending_deps; successors become Ready but transition immediately to Cancelled if their token is set. CancellationToken::child() clones the Arc<AtomicBool>, so cancelling the parent cancels all children simultaneously (use a fresh CancellationToken::new() for isolated subtree cancellation); Scope::spawn_with_cancel(f, token) accepts an explicit token, while plain Scope::spawn(f) inherits the scope-level token set via Scope::with_cancellation(token).

  • Panic propagation (pcs-worker, pcs-sync): worker run-loop wraps task.run() in std::panic::catch_unwind; on Err(payload) it sets task.state = Panicked and stores the payload in PanicCell: Mutex<Option<Box<dyn Any + Send>>>. Scope maintains panic_cell: Arc<PanicCell>; a child panic writes into it via try_lock + is_none (first-write-wins — later panics are logged but not stored, so only one panic re-raises per scope).

  • Scope::wait()/Scope::join(): once all children reach terminal state, a non-empty panic_cell triggers std::panic::resume_unwind(payload). Nested scopes each own a panic_cell; an inner scope's re-raise is caught by the outer scope's catch_unwind, re-stored in its own cell, and re-raised at its wait(). join(a, b) is a two-task Scope: push b, run a inline, pop-or-steal b, then check panic_cell after both complete. AssertUnwindSafe: task closures capturing non-UnwindSafe references must be wrapped in AssertUnwindSafe at spawn time (API contract, verified by a debug assertion at spawn).

enum ShutdownMode { DrainThenStop, AbortInFlight }
impl ThreadPool {
    fn shutdown(&self, mode: ShutdownMode);
    fn shutdown_with_timeout(&self, mode: ShutdownMode, timeout: Duration) -> Result<(), TimeoutError>;
    fn set_shutdown_mode(&mut self, mode: ShutdownMode);  // default before drop
}
  • DrainThenStop sets shutdown_flag: AtomicBool; workers finish the current task, drain remaining local-deque/Injector work (no new external submissions — submit()/try_submit() return Err(ShutdownError) immediately), and exit once deques are empty. AbortInFlight sets abort_flag: AtomicBool; workers check it after each task, transition remaining queued tasks to Cancelled without executing them, and exit after finishing the current in-progress task.
  • shutdown_with_timeout spawns a watchdog OS thread that auto-escalates DrainThenStop → AbortInFlight if workers are not all joined within timeout. Drop for ThreadPool calls shutdown(DrainThenStop) by default; panics inside shutdown tasks are caught via catch_unwind and the first payload is re-raised after all threads join.
  • Post-shutdown debug assertion: in_flight == 0 (checked in ThreadPool::drop, disabled in release builds); blocked submit() callers are woken via condvar.notify_all() on shutdown() and return Err(ShutdownError). Shutdown ordering: (1) set flag; (2) condvar.notify_all() for blocked submitters; (3) unpark() all parked workers; (4) join all JoinHandles in order.
  • No detached tasks (pcs-parallel, pcs-graph): Scope<'s>'s 's lifetime on Scope::spawn(f: impl FnOnce() + Send + 's) prevents closures from escaping, enforced by the borrow checker at compile time (no runtime check needed); TaskGraph's completion latch is likewise tied to the submitting scope via a Latch stored in the scope, so graph tasks cannot outlive their submitting scope's wait().

11.3 Deliverable

  • CancellationToken::cancel() causes all tasks sharing the token to skip execution and reach Cancelled state (verified by test_cancel_skips_tasks); a panicking child task causes scope.wait() to re-raise the panic on the calling thread with the original payload type. ThreadPool::shutdown(DrainThenStop) joins all threads with in_flight == 0 post-shutdown (verified by assertion); shutdown(AbortInFlight) exits promptly (≤ one max-task-duration) with cancelled tasks counted in tasks_cancelled_total; shutdown_with_timeout(mode, duration) auto-escalates DrainThenStop → AbortInFlight on timeout.
  • Scope<'s> lifetime prevents escaped closures at compile time (verified by compile-fail test); CancellationToken::child() and Scope::with_cancellation(token) are available in the pcs-sync public API; all conformance fixtures pass, all loom model checks for I7, I8, I10 pass without violations, and the pcs-cli benchmark parallel-bfs exercises task cancellation at scale, verifying no hung threads after AbortInFlight.

11.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Threshold
test_cancel_skips_tasks + test_cancelled_decrement 1000 tasks queued post-cancel; 0 execute, all reach Cancelled; each decrements in_flight and scope counter; in_flight == 0; scope completes normally
test_cancel_mid_execution cancel fired while tasks running; running tasks complete; queued tasks skip
test_panic_propagates + test_panic_payload_preserved panic in child → scope.wait() panics with same payload type; String re-raised as String, i32 as i32; no type erasure
test_nested_scope_panic inner scope panic propagates through outer scope boundary
test_drain_shutdown after DrainThenStop, in_flight == 0 and all threads joined (verified by JoinHandle)

11.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug signal Recovery
Panic payload dropped silently scope.wait() returns Ok(()) despite child panic catch_unwind Err not written to panic_cell Panicked state set but panic_cell None always write to panic_cell on Err; assert in test_panic_propagates
Cancelled task omits in_flight decrement in_flight never reaches 0; shutdown(Drain) hangs Cancelled code path skips the decrement in_flight gauge stuck > 0 post-drain decrement on every terminal state: Done, Panicked, Cancelled
resume_unwind inside Drop → double panic abort / process exit Scope::drop calls wait() which re-raises debugger: panic inside destructor move re-raise to explicit wait() only; Drop must catch_unwind and discard
child() token cancels sibling subtrees unexpectedly unrelated subtrees cancelled caller used child() when independent cancel intended unexpected Cancelled count in unrelated scope document: child() shares flag; use fresh CancellationToken::new() for isolation
Panic in shutdown cleanup code (not task) JoinHandle::join() returns Err non-task cleanup (deque flush) panics worker thread exits abnormally wrap all cleanup in catch_unwind; log; continue joining remaining threads
AssertUnwindSafe misuse logical data corruption post-unwind non-UnwindSafe data mutated partially then unwound data invariants broken silently document: closures capturing &mut must ensure logical invariants hold post-panic; prefer Mutex or RefCell

11.7 Performance Concerns

  • is_cancelled(): single Relaxed atomic load ≈ 0–1 ns; no cache miss if token is thread-local or task-local.
  • catch_unwind per task: ≈ 1–5 ns overhead on non-panic path (no exception in flight; verified by criterion micro; acceptable relative to minimum 1 µs task granularity); PanicCell's Mutex is acquired only on panic (rare path) via try_lock for first-write-wins, so it never touches the hot path.
  • DrainThenStop drain uses the same steal loop as normal operation (no additional synchronization; completes in O(remaining tasks / num_threads) time); its AbortInFlight escape hatch in shutdown_with_timeout avoids indefinite hangs at the cost of leaving tasks Cancelled rather than Done (metrics must distinguish).
  • CancellationToken::child() allocation: Arc::clone per child token; batch-create tokens for fine-grained DAGs to amortize allocation; pool from pcs-pool's token pool if hot path.
  • resume_unwind at scope boundary: one call per panicked scope, not a hot path; Scope::with_cancellation(token) stores Option<CancellationToken> in Scope with no cost when unset (no Arc cloned per spawn).

11.8 Edge Cases

  • cancel() called after all tasks complete: idempotent (AtomicBool already true or no remaining observers), no effect on completed tasks; an empty scope (no tasks spawned) has wait() return Ok(()) immediately with panic_cell None and cancelled_count == 0.
  • Panic with non-Send payload: payloads are Box<dyn Any + Send>; non-Send panics cannot cross thread boundary; emit log ERROR PanicPayloadNotSend { task_id }; transition to Panicked with None payload; caller sees Panicked with empty payload — document this limitation.
  • scope.wait() called from within a task in the same scope: deadlock if that task is the only thing that can complete other tasks; detect via thread-local SCOPE_DEPTH: Cell<u32> stack counter; return Err(RecursiveWait) or panic with message.
  • AbortInFlight during join(a, b): if b is mid-steal at abort time, steal completes; task transitions to Cancelled before execution; join re-raises ShutdownError (not the task panic).
  • CancellationToken created but never attached to a task: token holds one Arc reference; cancel() sets the flag; no tasks observe it; Arc freed when token dropped, no leak. Scope::with_cancellation(token) — a task spawned without an explicit token inherits the scope-level token, while spawn_with_cancel(f, different_token) overrides it for that task.
  • join(a, b) where a panics: b may still be running or pending; b's result is awaited before re-raising a's panic (ensures I4 — both complete before join returns).
  • shutdown_with_timeout expires before drain: transitions to AbortInFlight; tasks in DrainThenStop state that were draining become Cancelled; shutdown_aborted_total incremented; metrics record both drain duration and abort decision.

11.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Containment Recovery
Panic swallowed scope returns Ok despite child panic silent data loss / wrong results test_panic_propagates CI gate; tasks_panicked_total > 0 with no scope panic assert panic_cell checked on every wait() return; never return early
Shutdown hangs ThreadPool::drop blocks indefinitely process hang; no exit; OOM watchdog may fire in_flight stuck > 0 in metrics; watchdog alert use shutdown_with_timeout; escalate to AbortInFlight on timeout expiry
CancellationToken not polled by long task AbortInFlight slow to return; task_duration_ns > SLA shutdown delayed beyond deployment SLA task-duration histogram p99 alert require tasks to poll token at natural checkpoints; document as API contract in rustdoc
PanicCell payload held past scope (Arc cycle) heap grows with uncollected scopes; allocator watermark rises memory leak; eventual OOM heap profiler: PanicCell arc refcount > 1 after scope completes explicit drop(payload) in PanicCell after resume_unwind; scope drops Arc<PanicCell> on exit
scope.wait() double-called (compile-time escape) two threads call wait() on same scope race on panic_cell access; double resume_unwind Rust borrow checker prevents via &mut Scope signature on wait() wait() takes self by value or &mut self; Scope not Clone
resume_unwind with PoisonError from Mutex in cleanup pool cleanup panics; masks the original task panic both panics lost; process aborts debugger shows nested panic catch all panics in pool cleanup paths; log but do not propagate cleanup panics if task panic already in flight

Cross-phase interactions: Phase 4 (fork-join) — join(a,b) is a 2-task Scope, so panic propagation is natural with no separate join panic path; Phase 5 (DAG) — the completion latch is tied to the scope and GraphNode.panic_cell forwards to the scope's panic_cell, so wait() re-raises on a DAG-task panic; Phase 10 (backpressure) — shutdown() wakes blocked submit() callers with Err(ShutdownError), so the backpressure Condvar must be notified during shutdown; Phase 12 (blocking) — block_in_place(f) during DrainThenStop lets the task complete and the scope wait proceed, with f()'s panic propagated via the same catch_unwind path; Phase 13 (trace) — a Panicked span event carries task_id and payload_type, filterable in the flamegraph.

11.10 Operational Notes

  • pcs-metrics counters: tasks_cancelled_total, tasks_panicked_total, shutdown_drain_duration_ns histogram, shutdown_aborted_total, scope_panic_first_wins_total (increments when a second panic is discarded from a scope — useful for detecting multi-panic task groups where the first propagated panic is misleading). Panic payloads logged at ERROR level: message string if payload implements Display, else "<opaque type: {type_id}>".
  • CancellationToken propagation visible in pcs-trace task spans as cancelled=true span attribute (root cancel() recorded as a span event, filterable in flamegraph); operator procedure for a hung shutdown: inspect the in_flight gauge, identify stuck task IDs via trace spans with state=Running, and escalate to AbortInFlight via shutdown_with_timeout.
  • ShutdownMode selectable via set_shutdown_mode() before drop; default is DrainThenStop; deployment-specific override via POOL_SHUTDOWN_MODE env var (parsed at construction).
  • tasks_panicked_total rising unexpectedly → check task closure correctness; enable backtrace feature flag to capture stack traces in PanicCell and emit a trace span with a backtrace attribute; shutdown_drain_duration_ns histogram buckets are 1 ms, 10 ms, 100 ms, 1 s, 10 s, 60 s — values > 10 s indicate stuck tasks (alert threshold operator-configurable).
  • Cancellation audit: tasks_cancelled_total / tasks_submitted_total ratio > 5% sustained → caller cancelling too aggressively; review token lifetime and propagation scope.

Phase 12 — Blocking-Task Handling

12.1 Objectives

  • Detect when a worker is about to perform a blocking operation (explicit block_in_place(f) API), inject a compensation thread to keep the pool at num_threads active non-blocked workers, bound compensation threads via max_compensation: usize in PoolConfig to prevent unbounded OS thread creation, and prevent deadlock when all num_threads workers are blocked inside join / scope::wait waiting on tasks that have nowhere to run by implementing Latch::wait_with_work that executes tasks while waiting.
  • Constraints: compensation threads are transient — they exit when the blocking worker resumes; total OS threads ≤ num_threads + max_compensation at all times (I6 extended); P4 and P7 must hold. Phase scope: pcs-pool (block_in_place, CompensationPool, shutdown interaction), pcs-sync (Latch::wait_with_work), pcs-worker (WorkerState::Blocking, yield_to_scheduler); dependencies: Phase 1 (thread pool + topology), Phase 4 (join/Scope + Latch), Phase 9 (NUMA domains for affinity) must be complete.

Core invariant (I6 extended): non-blocked active workers ≤ num_threads at all times; compensation threads add up to max_compensation more, each active only while a permanent worker is blocked (P7: wait_with_work keeps waiting workers productive, so blocking callers don't starve other tasks). Full deadlock-prevention mechanism in §12.2 — Latch::wait_with_work keeps waiting workers active as thieves, with block_in_place compensation as fallback capacity; deadlock is only theoretically possible if both are exhausted and the caller's task graph has a dependency cycle (user error). wait_with_work must never be invoked from within a block_in_place context (would run tasks on a "blocked" worker, violating I1) — guarded by the IN_BLOCK_IN_PLACE flag.

12.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: P-B (Blumofe-Leiserson bound violation when workers block; compensation restores P-processor invariant); ADR-27 (compensation thread cap K×P); MM10 (compensation cures blocking, not overload); B9 Ch.27 (thread-pool saturation and deadlock analysis); I10 (shutdown drain with compensation threads); P4 (num_threads = physical cores) -->

Public API — pcs-pool:

fn block_in_place<F: FnOnce() -> R, R>(f: F) -> R;  // worker-thread only; debug-panics otherwise
fn yield_to_scheduler();  // soft yield: requeue continuation, run other tasks, return when deque empty
fn is_worker_thread() -> bool;  // true iff CURRENT_WORKER is Some; safe from any thread

  • block_in_place(f): signals the pool that the calling worker will block (increments active_comp, spawns a compensation thread if active_comp <= max_comp), calls f(), then decrements active_comp and signals the compensation thread to check for exit. Thread-local BLOCK_DEPTH: Cell<u32> guards re-entrant calls (increments active_comp only on the 0 → 1 transition; inner calls are transparent); it also sets worker.state = Blocking (visible to the compensation thread for its exit decision) before calling f, resetting to Running after return. yield_to_scheduler() pops and runs one task from the local deque (or steals one); if no work is found, returns immediately without parking — for cooperative yielding inside long-running tasks at natural checkpoints.

  • Compensation thread management (pcs-pool): CompensationPool embedded in ThreadPool: active_comp: AtomicUsize, max_comp: usize, parked_comp: Mutex<Vec<CompHandle>>; construction clones all Stealer handles from the pool (Arc-wrapped) and runs the standard worker steal loop using a freshly allocated Worker with id = COMP_BASE + index, named pcs-comp-N. On block_in_place entry, CAS-increment active_comp; if already at max_comp, log WARN CompensationExhausted { worker_id } and proceed without spawning (blocking still goes ahead).

  • Exit: on each steal-loop iteration the compensation thread checks whether active_comp has been decremented (blocking worker resumed) AND its local deque is empty AND steals fail, then exits and its handle is joined; on the block_in_place side, fetch_sub(1, Release) on active_comp unparks any parked compensation thread (or it detects and exits naturally on its next check). JoinHandles accumulate in parked_comp; the caller of block_in_place never joins them — ThreadPool::shutdown drains and joins all of parked_comp after joining permanent workers (already-exited handles join instantaneously). Called from outside the pool (CURRENT_WORKER = None): skip the CAS entirely, log DEBUG BlockInPlaceOutsidePool, and call f() directly.

  • Deadlock prevention (pcs-sync, pcs-worker): Latch::wait_with_work(worker: &Worker) replaces OS-blocking waits inside join paths: loop — if latch set, return; else pop a task from worker.local_lanes[highest]; if none, steal from a random victim; if steal fails, spin-wait with backoff (up to 1024 iterations), then park on an OS futex; wakens on unpark (new task or latch set).

  • join(a, b) pushes b to the local deque, runs a inline, then calls latch_b.wait_with_work(self) so the worker keeps executing tasks until b completes; a deadlock sentinel fires ERROR DeadlockSuspected if active_comp == 0 AND all workers are in wait_with_work spin AND injectors are empty AND no tasks are Running, panicking the pool with a state dump after a configurable deadlock_timeout (default 5 s). The condition requires user error (a mutual join dependency cycle) — the sentinel only aids debugging, running as a dedicated OS timer thread (not a pool worker) that checks every 100 ms, firing only when in_flight > 0 AND active_workers == 0 to avoid false positives during burst idle.

  • pcs-sched and NUMA integration: SchedPolicy::LocalityAware applies to compensation threads: NUMA domain copied from the blocking worker (blocking_worker.numa_node → CompensationWorker.numa_node) at spawn time via CpuSet::set_affinity; compensation threads never serve as work-stealing victims (the static victim list contains only original workers), they only steal.

Worker state machine extension:

Idle ─spawn→ Running ─block_in_place→ Blocking ─f() returns→ Running ─shutdown_flag set→ Exiting
Running ─steal_fail+backoff→ Parked ─unpark→ Running
Blocking ─abort_flag set→ Exiting (f() not interrupted; must complete naturally)
  • WorkerState gains a Blocking variant persisted in Worker.state: AtomicU8; Running ↔ Blocking transitions use Release stores, paired with an Acquire load in the compensation thread's exit check.

12.3 Deliverable

  • block_in_place(f) compiles and passes tests confirming a compensation thread is spawned and exits within 10 ms; pool maintains ≤ num_threads + max_compensation OS threads under test workload (verified by polling /proc/self/status or thread::count() equivalent); compensation thread names appear in the OS thread list as pcs-comp-N (verifiable by test introspection); join(a, b) where b depends on tasks from a's subtree completes without deadlock (integration test + loom model check).
  • Latch::wait_with_work is used in all join / Scope::wait paths, with no Latch::wait_os calls in fork-join critical paths; active_compensation_threads gauge exported in pcs-metrics; deadlock sentinel emits ERROR DeadlockSuspected in the constructed deadlock scenario within 5 s; is_worker_thread() returns true inside the pool and false outside, tested in both contexts; pcs-cli benchmark parallel-mergesort uses join recursively to completion on max_comp=0, demonstrating wait_with_work correctness at scale; block_in_place overhead benchmark measures entry/exit overhead (excluding f()) at ≤ 100 ns on the max_comp=0 fast path and ≤ 200 ns on the spawn path.

12.4 Acceptance Criteria

Check Threshold
test_block_in_place_compensates 1 thread blocks → 1 compensation spawned; N-1 tasks complete concurrently
test_max_compensation_limit max_comp=2, 10 concurrent block_in_place → ≤ 2 extra threads at peak (polled)
test_join_no_deadlock all N workers in join; nested tasks run via wait_with_work; all joins complete
test_compensation_exits_promptly blocking worker resumes → compensation exits within 10 ms (measured via comp_lifespan fixture)
test_comp_exhausted_warning max_comp=0, block_in_place called → WARN CompensationExhausted in trace; no panic
Thread count ceiling OS thread count ≤ num_threads + max_compensation (polled 100× over 1 s test window)
test_reentrant_block_in_place nested block_in_place (3 levels deep) → only 1 compensation spawned; BLOCK_DEPTH guard verified
test_deadlock_sentinel forced mutual-dependency deadlock → ERROR DeadlockSuspected emitted within 5 s
test_compensation_numa_affinity compensation thread runs on same NUMA domain as blocking worker (checked via /proc/self/status)
test_is_worker_thread returns true inside pool task, false from main thread; no panic in either context
test_block_in_place_during_shutdown AbortInFlight with active block_in_place: f() completes; worker exits; no hang
block_in_place_overhead criterion: overhead vs direct call ≤ 100 ns (excluding f() duration)

Fixture Purpose Expected
