outside_pool_block_in_place.txt block_in_place called from non-pool thread DEBUG BlockInPlaceOutsidePool logged; f() executes directly; no comp spawned; no panic

12.6 Pitfalls

Pitfall Manifestation Root cause Debug signal Recovery
Compensation thread outlives blocking period active_comp grows unboundedly over session compensation not notified on active_comp decrement active_comp gauge does not decrease ensure decrement via fetch_sub + unpark; assert active_comp returns to 0 in test
Deadlock via join + max_comp=0 all workers in join_wait; pool frozen wait_with_work not used in join path; workers park on OS futex all workers Running but no tasks completing; idle_ns maxes out enforce Latch::wait_with_work on all join paths; detect via sentinel
block_in_place from non-worker thread no compensation spawned; pool unaware thread not registered with pool; CURRENT_WORKER is None silent; throughput unaffected but no compensation check CURRENT_WORKER.is_some() before incrementing active_comp; log if called outside pool
Over-compensation race (comp spawned then immediately exits) brief active_comp spike; wasted thread spawn blocking worker returns before compensation starts active_comp spikes then immediately drops compensation checks active_comp == 0 at start; exits immediately if already zero
Stale Stealer after pool rebuild use-after-free or corruption in compensation steal pool rebuilt while compensations hold old stealers SIGSEGV or garbled task state Stealer wrapped in Arc; pool rebuild waits for active_comp == 0 before dropping old stealers
Nested block_in_place double-counts active_comp active_comp incremented twice for one blocking worker no re-entrant guard extra OS thread spawned; thread count exceeds ceiling BLOCK_DEPTH: Cell<u32> thread-local; increment active_comp only when 0 → 1

12.7 Performance Concerns

  • Compensation thread spawn latency: thread::spawn ≈ 5–50 µs on Linux, 10–200 µs on macOS — appropriate only for tasks blocking ≥ 1 ms; block_in_place is not suited to sub-millisecond blocking. wait_with_work spin loop is bounded at 1024 steal attempts before parking (CPU burn ≈ 1024 × steal_latency), acceptable for blocking durations ≥ 100 µs — equivalent cost to a normal steal loop during join, with better throughput than a futex sleep when tasks remain.
  • active_comp: AtomicUsize is modified only at block_in_place entry/exit (rare, not on the steal hot path); max_compensation = 0 fast path skips all compensation logic (single Relaxed load + branch, ≈ 1 ns overhead on the non-blocking path).
  • NUMA affinity for compensation: one-time set_affinity syscall at thread start, ≈ 1–5 µs, negligible relative to spawn latency; thread-name format! allocation is one heap allocation per compensation spawn, not on the hot path; compensation JoinHandle storage (Mutex<Vec<CompHandle>>) is touched only at spawn/exit, using try_lock on exit to avoid blocking the joining worker.

12.8 Edge Cases

  • block_in_place at shutdown: under DrainThenStop, compensation may not spawn if active_comp is at max, but the blocking task still executes and shutdown waits for it to return; under AbortInFlight, the worker transitions to Blocking and abort_flag is set, but f() is NOT interrupted (no cooperative cancel inside f) — the worker finishes f(), then checks the abort flag and exits.
  • All tasks blocking (pathological): pool degrades to max_compensation + num_threads threads, mostly blocked; P4 is intentionally breached — documented in PoolConfig API docs with a recommendation to use a dedicated async executor for I/O-heavy workloads.
  • Zero-duration block_in_place: a compensation thread may spawn before the blocking call returns and then exit immediately on its first steal-fail (acceptable — not intended for sub-microsecond operations); compensation thread panics are caught by catch_unwind in comp_loop, payload logged at ERROR, active_comp decremented so the next block_in_place can spawn a fresh one, and the pool continues.
  • yield_to_scheduler is disallowed inside block_in_place (thread-local IN_BLOCK_IN_PLACE flag makes it a no-op with a debug-assertion warning); join_wait with an empty pool (nothing to steal, no block_in_place in progress): spin limit hit → parks on OS futex via Latch::wait_os, woken when the latch is set (correct but slowest path) — if instead all num_threads workers are blocked in block_in_place with max_comp=0, the §12.2 sentinel detects the same deadlock, no self-healing, operator must restart the pool.
  • NUMA affinity staleness: if the blocking worker migrates domains between the block_in_place call and compensation spawn, the compensation thread uses the stale (immutable-after-construction) blocking_worker.numa_node — an acceptable approximation; multiple block_in_place calls from the same worker in different fibers/async tasks do not apply (the pool uses OS threads, not fibers), so each OS thread has its own BLOCK_DEPTH.

Interaction matrix — block_in_place with other phases:

Phase Interaction Resolution
Phase 5 (DAG) DAG node in block_in_place; successor cannot run compensation runs successor; I5 preserved
Phase 9 (NUMA) compensation inherits blocking worker's domain CpuSet::set_affinity at comp start
Phase 11 (cancel) cancel not checked inside f(); token checked before block document: poll token manually inside f()
Phase 13 (trace) block_in_place emits block_start/block_end span events span includes blocking_worker_id and comp_thread_id

12.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Containment Recovery
Deadlock: all workers OS-blocked in join pool frozen; no task completion; watchdog timeout all compute halted deadlock sentinel → ERROR DeadlockSuspected + state dump ensure Latch::wait_with_work on all join paths; sentinel triggers AbortInFlight
Thread exhaustion: OS thread limit reached thread::spawn returns Err(Os(11)); compensation spawn fails blocking tasks serialized; latency spikes unboundedly comp_spawn_failed_total counter alert decrease max_compensation; reduce blocking in tasks; use async runtime for I/O
Compensation thread leak OS thread count grows monotonically across pool lifetime eventual OOM or EAGAIN on new spawn active_comp gauge non-decreasing; OS thread count gauge add lifecycle assertion to CI; active_comp == 0 after all block_in_place calls complete
Stale Stealer crash SIGSEGV or garbled task execution rare; only during pool reconfiguration SIGSEGV in steal path; Rust UB possible without Arc protection Stealer wrapped in Arc; pool reconfiguration awaits active_comp == 0 before releasing old stealers
Deadlock in wait_with_work spin CPU at 100% with no task completions wastes CPU cycles; delays forward progress bounded spin (1024); then park on OS futex verify park+unpark path in loom model check; ensure latch-setter always unparks
block_in_place from non-pool thread causes silent throughput loss pool under-utilizes cores during blocking reduced throughput; no error surfaced is_worker_thread() check; DEBUG BlockInPlaceOutsidePool log document API contract; add debug assert; callers outside pool use explicit thread spawn
Double active_comp decrement active_comp wraps around (underflow) undefined pool behavior; future spawns unconstrained active_comp gauge drops below 0 (detect via signed comparison or saturation) BLOCK_DEPTH guard prevents; add assert(active_comp > 0) before fetch_sub in debug

12.10 Operational Notes

  • pcs-metrics exports: active_compensation_threads gauge, comp_exhausted_total, comp_spawn_failed_total, block_in_place_duration_ns histogram (buckets 1 ms/10 ms/100 ms/1 s/10 s — values > 1 s indicate a task unsuited to block_in_place), deadlock_suspected_total, wait_with_work_steal_count. max_compensation recommended default: num_threads / 2 — higher trades more OS threads/memory for parallelism under blocking, lower trades serialization for fewer threads; alert if active_compensation_threads > max_compensation * 0.8 sustained > 10 s (tasks blocking too frequently — refactor to async I/O or raise max_comp).
  • block_in_place API contract (rustdoc): intended for I/O waits, mutex contention, FFI blocking calls — not CPU-bound work (use spawn instead); it is not cancellable, and long operations should check is_cancelled() within f manually (interacts with Phase 11).
  • Deadlock sentinel runbook: on ERROR DeadlockSuspected, inspect pcs-trace for workers in Blocking or join_wait, then trigger shutdown(AbortInFlight) to recover; compensation thread IDs appear in pcs-trace spans with a comp_thread=true attribute, distinguishable from permanent workers in flamegraph export, and block_in_place calls from a non-worker thread log DEBUG BlockInPlaceOutsidePool (debug builds only, no panic in release).
  • CompensationPool cleanup on ThreadPool::drop: iterates parked_comp and joins all remaining compensation threads before the main pool joins permanent workers, ensuring no detached threads remain.

Phase 13 — Observability

13.1 Objectives

  • Expose scheduling telemetry (steal counts, idle time, queue depth, task latency) with low runtime overhead.
  • Emit per-task spans compatible with tracing subscriber ecosystem.
  • Produce scheduling-latency histograms and deque-depth gauges queryable at runtime.
  • Export task-tree flamegraphs as SVG via pcs-trace.
  • Toggle detailed tracing via a sampling rate knob; baseline metrics always-on at ~1 ns/task overhead.

13.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: S9 (Prometheus metrics exposition format); B8 (Systems Performance, USE method — utilization/saturation/errors per resource); B7 (BPF flamegraph methodology, Brendan Gregg); R7 (Inferno flamegraph format); ADR-26 (metrics sampling rate vs overhead); ADR-27 (tracing ring buffer drop policy); MM3 (T1/T∞ parallelism — visible in steal-rate metric) -->

pcs-metrics (src/metrics.rs):

Metric Storage Update site
steals_attempted AtomicU64 per-worker steal loop entry
steals_won AtomicU64 per-worker steal CAS success
tasks_run AtomicU64 per-worker task completion
park_count AtomicU64 per-worker park call
idle_ns AtomicU64 per-worker park duration
queue_depth AtomicI64 per-worker push +1 / pop -1
task_latency_ns HDR histogram, per-worker spawn-to-run delta
  • StealStats struct: aggregates per-worker atomics into a snapshot; fn snapshot(&self) -> SchedMetrics.
  • SchedMetrics struct: pool-wide totals + per-worker breakdown, serializable to JSON.
  • Histogram implementation: use a fixed-size power-of-2 bucket array (64 buckets, 1 µs–10 s range); no heap per task, only atomic increments.
  • Expose fn pool_metrics(pool: &ThreadPool) -> SchedMetrics; callable from any thread.

pcs-trace (src/trace.rs, src/flamegraph.rs):

  • TaskSpan { task_id: TaskId, worker_id: u32, spawn_ns: u64, start_ns: u64, end_ns: u64, parent_id: Option<TaskId>, stolen: bool }.
  • Ring buffer per worker (lock-free, power-of-2 size, overwrite on overflow); configurable size.
  • Sampling: AtomicU32 sample_counter; emit span iff (counter % sample_rate) == 0.
  • Flamegraph export: walk task-parent chain, aggregate CPU time by call path, emit Brendan Gregg folded-stacks format, pipe to inferno-flamegraph or write raw .folded file.
  • TracingGuard RAII type: records start_ns on construction, emits span on drop.
  • Integration with tracing crate: optional feature tracing-compat; emit tracing::span! events from worker loop without mandatory dependency.

13.3 Deliverable

  • pcs-metrics and pcs-trace crates compile; ThreadPool::metrics() returns live stats.
  • cargo run -p pcs-cli -- bench --metrics prints steal rate, idle%, task throughput.
  • cargo run -p pcs-cli -- bench --flamegraph out.folded produces flamegraph-ready output.
  • Histogram snapshot serializes to JSON via serde.

13.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
Metric overhead ≤ 2 ns/task on x86_64 (measured via microbench)
Steal counter accuracy matches manual counted baseline in deterministic mode
Histogram correctness bucket counts sum to tasks_run
Flamegraph completeness ≥ 95% of tasks have valid parent linkage
Tracing disabled overhead ≤ 1 ns/task (sampling gate only)
pool_metrics() from main thread no data race (verified under loom)

13.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture file Purpose Expected output
fixtures/metrics_zero_steal.toml single-worker pool; verify steals_won == 0 steals_won=0, tasks_run=N
fixtures/metrics_all_stolen.toml 1 producer thread, N-1 idle stealers, short burst steals_won ≈ task_count
fixtures/histogram_bucket_coverage.toml tasks with known latency ranges buckets populated in correct range
fixtures/flamegraph_tree.folded reference folded stacks for 3-level fork-join diff against golden file
fixtures/metrics_overflow_ring.toml ring buffer at capacity; verify no crash, metrics consistent no panic, overflow count incremented

13.6 Pitfalls

  • Timestamp skew across workers: CLOCK_MONOTONIC_RAW vs CLOCK_MONOTONIC differ per-core on VM; use std::time::Instant mapped to a single epoch at startup.
  • Histogram saturation silent: if all tasks exceed the max bucket, all land in the overflow bucket silently; add explicit overflow counter.
  • Ring buffer overwrite loses causal spans: a flamegraph missing a parent silently flattens the call tree; emit a span_dropped counter to detect.
  • Sampling bias at fixed rate: always-sampled first N tasks make cold-start appear faster than steady-state; use randomized skip (XorShift) not a fixed counter.
  • pool_metrics() torn read: reading per-worker atomics sequentially from an external thread gives a non-atomic snapshot; document as best-effort; add a snapshot_version to detect races.
  • tracing-compat feature causes dependency blowup: gate behind a Cargo feature; never enable by default in library crate.

13.7 Performance Concerns

  • Histogram bucket increments: single fetch_add on a thread-local shard avoids cross-core traffic; consolidate only on snapshot.
  • Ring buffer false sharing: pad TaskSpan entries to 64 bytes or pack into cache-line-aligned slots.
  • Flamegraph walk is O(N log N) sort + O(N) fold; run offline (export only), never on hot path.
  • idle_ns accumulation: record park_start in thread-local, add delta on unpark; avoids atomic CAS.
  • Sampling gate: a single branch on a thread-local counter; predicted-not-taken in steady state.

13.8 Edge Cases

  • Worker spawns a task with parent_id = None (external submission via Injector): flamegraph roots it at the synthetic [external] node.
  • Pool shut down before all ring-buffer spans are flushed: drain ring buffers in shutdown hook before joining threads.
  • Metrics requested on a pool with 0 workers (e.g., deterministic mode): return zeroed SchedMetrics without panic.
  • Task panics before span drop: TracingGuard::drop must be panic-safe; record end_ns and set panicked=true flag.
  • Clock wraps on 32-bit targets (hypothetical): all timestamps are u64 ns; wrap not reachable in practice but document assumption.

13.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery
Ring buffer corruption (wrong index) assertion on head/tail mod mask one worker's spans lost disable trace, continue metrics
Histogram bucket off-by-one fixture mismatch wrong latency report fix bucket boundary formula, replay
steals_won > steals_attempted invariant check on snapshot misleading stats reset counters, investigate memory ordering
Flamegraph export OOM allocator failure during fold export abort cap max spans exported; stream to file

13.10 Operational Notes

  • Enable metrics at any verbosity with RUST_LOG=pcs_metrics=debug; structured JSON lines to stderr.
  • Flamegraph export: ThreadPool::export_flamegraph(path) blocks briefly to drain ring buffers.
  • Sampling rate tunable at runtime via PoolConfig::trace_sample_rate: u32; 0 = off, 1 = all, N = 1-in-N.
  • Integration with Prometheus: SchedMetrics implements Display in OpenMetrics text format (feature-gated).
  • No external daemon required; all telemetry in-process.

Phase 14 — Determinism mode + bench + conformance

14.1 Objectives

  • Implement single-threaded deterministic replay mode for debugging non-deterministic scheduling bugs; provide a criterion-based benchmark suite covering scaling 1..N cores, speedup curves, and steal-rate analysis.
  • Conformance fixture suite: parallel results match sequential reference for all data-parallel primitives; run loom model-checker on the Chase-Lev deque (pcs-deque) covering all memory orderings.
  • Soak test: multi-hour runs at full parallelism; detect leaks, starvation, deadlock.

14.2 Detailed Implementation Steps

<!-- Theory anchors: R9 (loom model checker, exhaustive interleaving); B6 (Rust atomics/locking, Ch.9 loom integration); ADR-18 (loom scope: pcs-deque only, not full pool); P-O (Musuvathi CHESS deterministic scheduler); MM1 (steal-rate metric validates theoretical contention model); P-B (Blumofe-Leiserson linear-speedup conformance at ≥4 cores); I1–I10 (all invariants validated in final soak) -->
  • Deterministic mode (pcs-pool/src/deterministic.rs): DeterministicPool runs a single worker on the calling thread; spawn pushes to a VecDeque<Task> (no atomics, no threading); execution order is LIFO (matches owner-pop semantics), reproducing the work-first schedule without stealing.
  • Seed: PoolConfig::deterministic: bool; when true, ThreadPool::new returns a DeterministicPool behind the same trait object; task IDs are assigned sequentially from a u64 counter, reproducible across runs.
  • Panic propagation identical to parallel mode: captures payload, re-raises at scope boundary; no Arc/Mutex in the hot path — plain Vec and stack recursion.

Criterion benches (pcs-cli/benches/):

Benchmark Parameter sweep Measured metric
parallel_mergesort N = 1..16 workers, array 10M u64 wall time, speedup vs 1-thread
matrix_multiply N = 1..16, 512×512 f64 GFLOP/s, efficiency
bfs_graph N = 1..16, 1M-node random graph edges/sec, steal rate
nbody_step N = 1..16, 100K bodies ns/body, idle%
steal_rate fixed workload, vary granularity steals/task
injector_throughput external submit rate tasks/sec, queue depth
  • Each bench uses criterion_group! with sample_size(20), measurement_time(Duration::from_secs(10)); output is CSV + plotters PNG stored in target/criterion/, and CI asserts no regression > 10%.

Conformance fixtures (fixtures/conformance/):

Fixture Parallel primitive Verification
reduce_sum_f64.toml parallel reduce, + abs(par - seq) < epsilon * N
map_square.toml parallel map element-wise equality
scan_prefix_sum.toml parallel scan prefix equality to sequential
parallel_for_side_effect.toml parallel_for with atomic counter final counter == N
dag_diamond.toml 4-node diamond DAG completion order valid, result correct
dag_chain_100.toml 100-node chain all nodes execute in dependency order
scope_nested.toml 3-level nested scopes no task escapes scope lifetime
panic_propagate.toml task panics in scope outer thread catches exactly one panic

  • loom model-check (pcs-deque/tests/loom_deque.rs): covers concurrent push + steal, concurrent pop + steal, and grow triggered mid-steal, using 2 workers (owner + 1 thief) and 3 tasks under exhaustive loom::model.

  • Asserts no double-execution (I1), no lost task (I3), no data race on buffer slots; CI runs LOOM_MAX_BRANCHES=100000 cargo test --test loom_deque.

  • Soak test (pcs-cli/src/soak.rs): a 4-hour run mixing fork-join, DAG, parallel_for, and random granularity, checking every 60 s for no leaked Pending tasks, stable resident memory (< 5% growth/hour), and no worker thread exit, with panics injected randomly every 10 s to verify propagation and recovery.

14.3 Deliverable

  • cargo test --test loom_deque passes under loom with zero model-check violations; cargo bench -p pcs-cli produces speedup curves for all 4 benchmark kernels.
  • All conformance fixtures pass (cargo test --test conformance); DeterministicPool reproduces identical task execution order on repeated runs (seed-independent because purely LIFO sequential).
  • Soak test runs 4 h without panic, memory growth, or stalled workers.

14.4 Acceptance Criteria

Criterion Target
Speedup on mergesort (8 cores) ≥ 5.5× vs 1-thread
Speedup on matrix multiply (8 cores) ≥ 6.0×
Steal rate bench regression gate ≤ 10% wall-time increase vs baseline
loom: branches explored ≥ 10,000 per test configuration
Conformance: reduce_sum_f64 abs(par - seq) < 1e-6 * N
Conformance: all others bitwise or element-wise equality
Soak: memory growth < 5% RSS increase over 4 h
Soak: worker exits 0 unexpected exits
DeterministicPool reproducibility 100/100 identical task-ID sequences

14.5 Conformance Fixtures

Fixture file Purpose Malformed / edge case
reduce_sum_f64.toml float associativity documented N=0 → identity value; N=1 → single element
scan_prefix_sum.toml prefix scan correctness N=0 → empty vec; N=1 → single pass-through
dag_cycle_rejected.toml DAG cycle detection expect Err(CycleDetected)
scope_nested.toml lifetime enforcement compile-fail test for escaping borrow
panic_propagate.toml panic re-raise exactly one std::panic::catch_unwind wrapping scope
loom_deque_grow.toml ABA + grow under loom no assertion fail in loom model
soak_config.toml soak parameters configurable duration, panic injection rate
bench_regression_baseline.json criterion baseline CI diff; fail if > 10% regression

14.6 Pitfalls

  • loom pitfalls: state-space explosion — 3+ concurrent actors with fetch_add can exceed the default branch limit (constrain to 2-worker + small task count, use loom::sync::atomic not std::sync::atomic); false-negative — loom requires using its thread::spawn, since std::thread::spawn inside pcs-deque tests silently bypasses model checking (audit imports in the test file).
  • Benchmark pitfalls: criterion warm-up too short on NUMA — allocator state differs cold vs warm, force at least 2 s warm-up and pin the benchmark to a single NUMA domain for reproducibility; background steals influence results — run benches with taskset/cpuset isolation (documented in pcs-cli/benches/README).
  • Conformance pitfalls: DeterministicPool silently diverges from parallel semantics if parallel_for's internal rayon-style split logic isn't mirrored exactly, breaking task-ID conformance; float reduce reorders additions — document in the fixture header that tolerance is expected, and never assert bitwise equality for f32/f64 reduce.
  • Soak test masks slow leaks: allocator fragmentation looks like growth; measure via /proc/self/smaps (Linux) or mach_task_basic_info (macOS), not just RSS; add a 15-min linear regression check.

14.7 Performance Concerns

  • DeterministicPool allocates a VecDeque per scope; for deeply nested scopes this is O(depth) allocs (use a thread-local slab); criterion black_box must wrap all outputs to prevent dead-code elimination of benchmark kernels.
  • loom tests are 100–1000× slower than unit tests; run only in the --test loom_deque target, never in cargo test --all.
  • Soak memory check (smaps parsing, ~1 ms) is acceptable at 60 s intervals but should not be inlined into the hot scheduler loop; flamegraph generation during a bench run distorts results, so tracing must be disabled when running criterion (sample_rate=0).

14.8 Edge Cases

  • DeterministicPool with N=0 tasks in a scope: scope completes immediately, no panic; criterion bench with num_threads=0: PoolConfig validation rejects, bench returns Err and skips rather than panicking.
  • loom test with grow on the deque: buffer pointer swap; loom must see the acquire-release pair, verified using loom::cell::UnsafeCell for the buffer.
  • Conformance dag_cycle_rejected: cycle detection must run at graph-build time, not at execution; ensure TaskGraph::add_dependency returns Err without spawning.
  • Soak panic injection into a task that holds a Barrier: Barrier must not deadlock remaining waiters (Barrier::wait checks a poisoned flag); float scan with N not a power of 2: splitter must handle uneven halves, verified so no elements are skipped.

14.9 Failure Analysis

Failure Detection Blast radius Recovery
loom assertion: double-execute I1 violated in model deque unsafe fix memory ordering; re-run
Criterion regression > 10% CI gate blocked merge profile steal path; check cache alignment
Conformance mismatch on reduce fixture diff wrong results in production audit splitter + reduce combiner
Soak deadlock all workers stuck > 30 s pool frozen dump worker states; inspect Barrier poison flag
DeterministicPool task ID divergence reproducibility test fails 1/100 debug session useless audit TaskId counter reset between runs
Memory leak in soak RSS growth > 5%/h eventual OOM attach heaptrack; check ring-buffer or VecDeque growth

14.10 Operational Notes

  • Run loom checks in CI with RUSTFLAGS="--cfg loom" and a dedicated loom feature gate (never compile loom into release builds); criterion baseline JSON is committed to the repo under pcs-cli/benches/baselines/, updated by cargo bench -- --save-baseline.
  • Deterministic mode activated via env var PCS_DETERMINISTIC=1 or PoolConfig::deterministic = true; no code-path change required by callers.
  • Soak test run via cargo run -p pcs-cli -- soak --hours 4 --metrics-interval 60 (outputs metrics CSV); conformance suite via cargo test -p pcs-cli --test conformance -- --nocapture, with each fixture printing pass/fail plus actual-vs-expected diff.
  • Benchmark isolation: document the taskset -c 0-7 cargo bench invocation in pcs-cli/README.md; CI runs on a dedicated bare-metal runner, not a shared VM.

Threat Model

Trust Boundary Map

[User Code] --> spawn / scope / graph-submit / parallel_for
                       |
        +--------------+---------------------------+
        |       pcs-pool boundary                  |
        |   ThreadPool / Injector (global MPMC)    |
        |   PoolConfig validation                  |
        |                                          |
        |  Worker 0 ... Worker N-1                 |
        |  (same process / UID; share heap)        |
        |   Chase-Lev deques, steal loops          |
        +--------------+---------------------------+
               |                   |
  [pcs-numa: sched_setaffinity / hwloc]   [pcs-trace / pcs-metrics]
               |                                   |
   [OS: /sys/devices/system/cpu]        [trace ring-buffer -> file/fd]
                                                   |
                                            [operator / dashboard]

Trust boundaries:

  • User code and worker threads: same process / address space; closures are opaque but trusted (user code is not adversarial by default).
  • pcs-pool boundary: PoolConfig is validated at ThreadPool::build(); oversized num_threads clamped; malformed graphs (cycles, duplicate node IDs) rejected before enqueueing.
  • OS affinity syscalls: narrow external boundary via sched_setaffinity / hwloc; failures logged at WARN, silently skipped; correctness not affected.
  • Trace/metrics output: write-only boundary to operators via file or pull endpoint; no inbound data path from trace consumers back into scheduler.
  • No network surface; no IPC; no persistent disk state between pool lifetimes.
  • pcs-cli benchmarks: separate binary; no trust boundary with the library; cannot affect scheduler state of other processes.

Threat Severity Matrix

Severity Threats Impact Likelihood
Critical T3 (double-exec), T11 (memory corruption), T5 (deadlock) Correctness violation or hang Low (prevented by loom/Miri); Medium (deadlock without Phase 12)
High T1 (panic), T4 (starvation), T7 (memory exhaustion) Liveness failure or OOM Medium
Medium T6 (cycle), T12 (misconfiguration), T13 (cancellation non-compliance) Functional error or latency Medium
Low T2 (ABA), T8 (affinity), T9 (supply-chain), T10 (trace DoS), T14 (NUMA fallback), T15 (lifetime) Performance degradation or data loss (trace only) Low

Threat Table

ID Threat Attack / Bug Vector Mitigation Detection Telemetry Recovery
T1 Buggy/panicking closure corrupts scheduler state `spawn( unsafe { *ptr = 0 })` — panic unwinds through task run TaskState atomic: Pending→Running→Panicked; std::panic::catch_unwind per task (Phase 3); scheduler state only touched before/after run tasks_panicked counter non-zero
T2 ABA on Chase-Lev deque top counter Steal wraps monotonic counter, old slot reused as new task Monotonic top (u64); never decremented; 64-bit space makes wrap ≈ impossible; loom model-check verifies no reuse loom CI; runtime: assert top never decreases deque_top_wrap_near (gauge) Widen type; compile-time size assert; fix memory ordering
T3 Double-execution of a task Owner pop and thief steal race; both succeed I1/I3: steal is single CAS on top; owner sees empty on concurrent steal; TaskState CAS from Pending→Running is exclusive steals_won vs tasks_run accounting; trace shows single run_start per TaskId double_exec_alarm (assert in debug) loom verification in Phase 14; Miri
T4 Task starvation / livelock — all workers parked with pending work Unpark signal lost between push and park check; spurious park park/unpark uses OS condvar; push-then-check order: check queue after parking; exponential backoff; watchdog timer force-unparks if queue_depth > 0 and all workers idle idle_ns spike + queue_depth nonzero park_count, idle_ns, queue_depth Watchdog: if condition holds > 50 ms, force-unpark all

T6 DAG cycle submitted by user User constructs cycle in TaskGraph; nodes never reach pending_deps == 0 Cycle detection on graph submission (DFS / topo-sort attempt); reject with typed Err before any tasks enqueue graph_cycle_reject counter graph_cycle_reject Return Err to caller; tasks not submitted; I5

T9 Supply-chain attack via malicious dependency Transitive crate in dependency graph Minimize external deps: only libc (affinity), optional hwloc binding, criterion (dev-only); Cargo.lock checked in; cargo-deny in CI (license + advisory) CI cargo-deny check gate cargo-deny advisory DB hits Pin versions; review all dep upgrade PRs
T10 Trace write amplification / DoS on storage Very high spawn rate with tracing enabled Sampling (configurable 1:N); ring-buffer backed async writer; auto-reduce sample rate if write latency exceeds 1 µs; trace_drops metric signals loss trace_drops counter spike; write latency P99 alert trace_drops, trace_write_ns_p99 Reduce sample rate; disable trace; RB-05
T11 Memory corruption via unsound unsafe in pcs-deque Incorrect memory orderings on buffer growth; missing fence Code review + loom model-check (Phase 14); Miri CI run on deque unit tests; release fence around buffer-swap pointer Miri PASS required in CI CI report Fix ordering; MSRV bump if needed; test with ASAN
T12 Operator misconfiguration (num_threads > logical CPUs) PoolConfig::num_threads set to 2 × available_parallelism PoolConfig::build() clamps to available_parallelism() with a WARN log; no silent oversubscription config_thread_clamp warning in logs config_thread_clamp Auto-clamp; emit WARNING; user should review
T13 Cancellation token ignored by long-running task Task never polls cancellation token; scope deadline exceeded Cooperative model; scope injects a wall-clock deadline; on timeout scope poisons itself; subsequent task spawns return Err scope_timeout_trigger counter scope_cancels, scope_timeout_ns Scope returns; ongoing task runs to natural exit; user must audit
T14 NUMA misidentification (VM, container, no hwloc) hwloc absent; VM reports wrong topology NUMA detection wrapped in fallback: if hwloc absent → single domain; log at INFO; correctness preserved numa_fallback_mode flag in logs numa_domains_detected Accept single-domain; performance degrades; document hwloc install
T15 Scope lifetime violation via unsafe transmute of 'scope lifetime User coerces Scope<'s> to 'static; spawns outliving data Rust lifetime on Scope<'s> prevents this in safe code; document that unsafe circumvention is UB; no mitigation beyond borrow checker Compile-time (safe code); doc warning (unsafe) N/A Do not accept task closures with 'static workaround in safe API

Failure Modes & Recovery

Failure Classification

Failures group into three classes:

  • Correctness failures: invariant violation (double-exec, lost task, wrong reduce result). Must not occur; caught by loom/Miri/conformance tests (Phase 14).
  • Liveness failures: pool makes no progress (deadlock, starvation, shutdown hang). Detected by metrics; automatic or operator-guided recovery.
  • Performance failures: throughput below SLO, steal rate degraded, NUMA misalignment. No data loss; remediated by tuning.

Failure Taxonomy

Class Symptoms Root Causes Detection Automatic Recovery Manual Recovery Operator Actions Forensic Artifacts Long-term Mitigation
Task panic Scope/join returns Err(PanicPayload); tasks_panicked > 0 Bug in user closure; stack overflow; unwinding FFI AtomicTaskState → Panicked; scope catch_unwind Scope re-raises at boundary (I7); other scope tasks unaffected Inspect panic message; reproduce in determinism mode (Phase 14) Extract TaskId from trace; correlate with input data Panic message + backtrace in trace span for that TaskId Require UnwindSafe closures; property-test closures with proptest

Operational Readiness

Metrics Strategy

All metrics are per-worker atomics (StealStats, SchedMetrics) with no global lock on the hot path (P8). Pool-level aggregation happens on pull, not push. Metric collection does not introduce any synchronization into the scheduler critical path. Atomic reads with Relaxed ordering suffice for counter accumulation; only blocked_workers gauge requires Acquire to observe correct state.

Key metric relationships for diagnosis:

  • steals_won / steals_attempted → steal hit rate; < 30% indicates tasks too fine-grained (P6 violation).
  • idle_ns / total_ns per worker → utilization; > 20% sustained under load indicates over-provisioning or starvation.
  • blocked_workers / num_threads → deadlock proximity; threshold at 80% triggers compensation thread.
  • task_latency_us P99 / P50 ratio → tail latency; ratio > 10× indicates stragglers or lock contention.
  • numa_cross_domain_steals / steals_won → NUMA locality quality; target < 20% cross-domain under balanced load.
Metric Type Source Crate Interpretation
tasks_run counter / worker pcs-metrics Throughput baseline; sum across workers
steals_attempted counter / worker pcs-metrics Steal pressure
steals_won counter / worker pcs-metrics Steal efficiency (won/attempted → hit rate)
park_count counter / worker pcs-worker Idleness frequency
idle_ns counter / worker pcs-worker Cumulative idle time per worker
queue_depth gauge / worker (sampled) pcs-deque Task backlog; imbalance indicator
injector_depth gauge pcs-pool External backpressure; OOM early warning
task_latency_us histogram pcs-metrics P50/P99/P999 scheduling latency (spawn→run_start)
steal_latency_ns histogram pcs-worker Steal overhead; must be << task cost
blocked_workers gauge pcs-worker Deadlock early warning
compensation_threads_spawned counter pcs-pool Blocking task frequency
trace_drops counter pcs-trace Tracing fidelity
affinity_bind_errors counter pcs-numa Topology degradation
numa_cross_domain_steals counter pcs-worker NUMA locality quality
numa_domains_detected gauge pcs-numa Topology detection success
tasks_panicked counter pcs-core Correctness alarm
submit_rejected counter pcs-pool Backpressure effectiveness
graph_cycle_reject counter pcs-graph Malformed DAG frequency
config_thread_clamp counter pcs-pool Misconfiguration frequency
scope_timeout_trigger counter pcs-parallel Cancellation compliance

Export: pull endpoint (Prometheus text format); also available via ThreadPool::metrics() -> SchedMetrics for programmatic embedding. Metrics snapshots can be written to disk by operator tooling; see Compatibility & Versioning for format versioning.

Granularity control metrics (Phase 7 feedback loop):

  • adaptive_split_depth_avg: average recursion depth before leaf task; used to auto-tune granularity.
  • steal_to_task_cost_ratio: estimated steal cost / average task cost; target < 0.01 (1%).
  • over_decomposition_events: number of times task was smaller than steal cost threshold; signals need to increase leaf size.

Tracing Strategy

  • Span events emitted per task: spawn, run_start, run_end, steal, park, unpark, panic, cancel, scope_enter, scope_exit, dag_node_ready.
  • Each span carries: TaskId, worker_id, numa_domain, wall_ns, parent_task_id (for scope/DAG trees).
  • Storage: in-process lock-free ring buffer (default 64 MB, circular, overwrite-oldest); async flush thread; export to JSON-ND or binary format.
  • Flamegraph: inferno-compatible SVG from task tree; generated on-demand via pcs-trace::export_flamegraph() or SIGUSR1.
  • Sampling rate: configurable 1:N (default 1:100 production, 1:1 in determinism mode Phase 14).
  • Overhead budget: < 50 ns per sampled event; unsampled: single branch + atomic counter increment.
  • Toggle at runtime: pcs_trace::set_enabled(bool) without pool restart.
  • Trace schema versioned with schema_version: u8 in binary header (see Compatibility section).

Structured Logging

Level Conditions
ERROR Deadlock detection; ABA alarm; invariant violation in debug build
WARN Affinity bind failure; NUMA fallback; config clamp; thread respawn; deprecated API use
INFO Pool start/stop; effective PoolConfig; topology summary; shutdown completion time
DEBUG Steal decisions; park/unpark events per worker; compensation thread lifecycle
TRACE Per-task state transitions; deque push/pop operations (dev-only; not for production)

Format: JSON-lines; mandatory fields: ts_ns (u64 monotonic), level, crate, worker_id (Option<usize>), task_id (Option<u64>), msg. No allocations on hot path; log calls gate on level check before format.

Dashboard Design

Four panels (reference metric names above):

Panel Key Metrics Normal Range Alert
Throughput tasks_run/s per worker + aggregate; steals_won/s Grows linearly with cores < 50% expected at given core count
Steal Efficiency steals_won / steals_attempted (hit rate); steal_latency_ns P99 Hit rate 60–90%; steal latency < 200 ns Hit rate < 30% (granularity issue)
Saturation idle_ns / total_ns per worker; park_count rate; blocked_workers < 10% idle under load Any worker idle > 20% sustained; blocked_workers > 0
Health tasks_panicked; submit_rejected; trace_drops; graph_cycle_reject; scope_timeout_trigger All zero Any non-zero (page on panicked/blocked)

Alerting Philosophy

  • Page on: blocked_workers == num_threads for > 5 s; tasks_panicked rate > 0 sustained; injector_depth growing unboundedly (OOM risk); worker_alive_count drops.
  • Warn on: steal hit rate < 30%; idle_ns > 20% of total on any worker under load; trace_drops > 0; submit_rejected > 0.
  • Do not alert on: transient steal misses; short parks in low-load periods; single isolated scope_timeout_trigger.

SLO Definitions

SLO Target Measurement
Parallel speedup (8-core, mergesort 10 M elements) ≥ 6× vs sequential criterion bench per commit
Steal hit rate under balanced load ≥ 60% 5-min rolling window
Task scheduling latency P99 (spawn→run_start, idle pool) < 5 µs histogram P99
Pool startup latency (N ≤ 64 threads) < 10 ms bench fixture
Graceful shutdown completion < 100 ms test fixture
Deque push/pop throughput (owner only) > 100 M ops/s single-thread micro-bench

Incident Response Procedures

  1. Deadlock (blocked_workers == num_threads): collect thread dump (SIGQUIT); check if compensation threads were spawned (compensation_threads_spawned counter); identify join chain in thread stacks; annotate blocking tasks with blocking_scope; increase max_compensation_threads in PoolConfig; restart pool if needed → RB-01.
  2. Panic storm (tasks_panicked rate rising): extract TaskId from trace spans; reproduce in determinism mode (Phase 14, single-thread replay); inspect panic payload; fix closure; note pool remains live and scope callers receive Err without pool restart → RB-02.
  3. Throughput collapse (tasks_run/s → 0 with queue_depth rising): distinguish starvation (all parked: blocked_workers == 0, park_count spiking) vs deadlock (all blocked: blocked_workers == num_threads); apply watchdog force-unpark for starvation path; inject compensation for deadlock path; verify PoolConfig num_threads not clamped below expectation → RB-03.
  4. Memory growth (RSS climbing, submit_rejected rising): profile with DHAT / heaptrack; verify backpressure max_in_flight is set; check for unbounded recursive spawn (measure adaptive_split_depth_avg); increase min_task_size leaf threshold → RB-04.
  5. Tracing data loss (trace_drops > 0): reduce sample rate via pcs_trace::set_sample_rate(); increase ring buffer size in PoolConfig; measure disk I/O throughput; disable trace entirely if unacceptable; accept partial trace for post-incident analysis → RB-05.
  6. NUMA locality degradation (numa_cross_domain_steals / steals_won > 20%): verify hwloc detects correct topology (numa_domains_detected metric); check container cpuset configuration; adjust worker-to-domain assignment; consider per-domain injectors (Phase 9) → RB-06.
  7. SLO breach: speedup < 6×: profile with criterion bench; check steal hit rate and granularity metrics; verify NUMA pinning; check if compensation threads are inflating the apparent thread count; review PoolConfig policy (try LocalityAware) → ad hoc.

Pre-Incident Checklists

Pool startup verification:

  • numa_domains_detected matches expected topology
  • affinity_bind_errors == 0 (or accepted degraded mode documented)
  • config_thread_clamp == 0 (num_threads not silently reduced)
  • INFO log shows effective PoolConfig (num_threads, stack_size, policy, affinity)

Steady-state health gates (CI integration test assertions):

  • steals_won / steals_attempted >= 0.6 under mergesort benchmark
  • idle_ns / total_ns < 0.1 per worker under saturating workload
  • tasks_panicked == 0 after all conformance fixtures
  • Pool shutdown completes in < 100 ms after draining test workload

Runbooks

ID Trigger Key Steps
RB-01 blocked_workers == num_threads for > 5 s Thread dump → identify root join chain → add blocking_scope annotation → restart pool → verify blocked_workers drops
RB-02 tasks_panicked > 0 sustained Extract panic TaskId from trace → reproduce in determinism mode → fix closure → redeploy
RB-03 tasks_run/s drops > 50%, queue_depth rising Check blocked_workers: if 0 → starvation (force-unpark) → if > 0 → deadlock (RB-01) → verify PoolConfig num_threads → measure steal rate
RB-04 RSS growing unboundedly; submit_rejected rising Measure injector_depth trend → reduce submission rate → increase granularity floor → reduce task fan-out depth → profile heap
RB-05 trace_drops > 0 Reduce sample rate → increase ring buffer size → check disk I/O → disable trace if unacceptable → adjust retention policy
RB-06 numa_cross_domain_steals high; latency elevated Verify hwloc installation → check container --cpuset-cpus → rebalance workers to NUMA domains → restart pool
RB-07 affinity_bind_errors > 0 Check container capabilities (CAP_SYS_NICE) → accept degraded-mode (correctness intact) → document in deployment notes
RB-08 Process does not exit within 200 ms of shutdown Broadcast cancellation token → force-join threads after timeout → inspect stuck TaskIds in trace → identify task holding resource
RB-09 graph_cycle_reject non-zero Log submitted edge list → identify cycle in user code → fix DAG construction → verify with sequential topo-sort test

Trace / Artifact Management

  • Ring buffer: fixed-size (default 64 MB), overwrite-oldest; flush to file on SIGUSR1 or explicit pcs_trace::flush() API call.
  • Retention policy: trace files rotated daily, kept 7 days; metrics snapshots kept 30 days; flamegraph SVGs kept per-incident (tagged with incident ID).
  • Flamegraph export: written to $TMPDIR/pcs-flame-<pid>-<ts>.svg on SIGUSR1 or export_flamegraph() call; also exported automatically on scope panic if tracing is enabled.
  • Trace file naming: pcs-trace-<pid>-<date>T<time>Z.bin; JSON-ND mirror at .json.nd extension.
  • No persistent scheduler state between pool lifetimes; all state is in-process; trace files are the sole durable artifact.
  • Trace integrity: each flush appends a CRC32 frame footer; readers verify before deserializing; truncated frames skipped with a truncated_frame warning.

Capacity Planning

Dimension Sizing Rule
Worker threads available_parallelism() − 1 (leave one core for OS + main thread)
Ring buffer 64 MB default; 1 MB per 1000 tasks/s at 1:100 sample rate
Compensation threads max 2× num_threads; bounded to prevent over-subscription
Injector depth num_threads × 64 tasks as default backpressure limit
Stack size 2 MB default (Rust default); increase for deep recursive divide-and-conquer

Deployment Topology

  • pcs is a library (not a service); deployed as a crate dependency in the host binary.
  • One ThreadPool per process: standard topology; multiple pools are supported but compete for NUMA domains.
  • Container: set --cpuset-cpus to a contiguous NUMA-local CPU set; mount /sys/devices/system/cpu read-only for topology detection; install hwloc in the image.
  • VM: verify vCPU topology in lscpu --json; confirm NUMA node count and cache sizes match expected.
  • Physical: bind process to NUMA nodes with numactl --membind=0 --cpunodebind=0; let pcs-numa do per-worker pinning.

Canary / Rolling Upgrade / Rollback

  • Library canary: ship new crate version to subset of application instances; compare steal hit rate and latency SLOs across cohorts.
  • Rolling upgrade: update Cargo.toml crate version; rebuild; redeploy in waves; monitor SLO panels between waves; pause if alert fires.
  • Rollback: revert Cargo.lock to prior version; rebuild; redeploy; no persistent scheduler state to migrate.
  • Breaking PoolConfig changes: deprecated fields kept with adapter shim for one MINOR version; removed at next MAJOR.

Compatibility & Versioning

Semantic Versioning Policy

MAJOR.MINOR.PATCH applied per published crate. All crates in the workspace version together (single workspace version).

Bump Trigger
PATCH Bug fix; no API, wire, or format change
MINOR New optional API field or method; additive StealStats fields; new trace event type; new SchedPolicy variant (#[non_exhaustive]); new PoolConfig optional field with Default impl
MAJOR Breaking change to Task, TaskId, ChaseLevDeque public API; Scope<'s> lifetime contract change; trace binary format incompatible; SchedMetrics field removal; TaskState variant removal

API Stability Tiers

Tier Crates / Types Guarantee
Stable pcs-pool: ThreadPool, PoolConfig, spawn, shutdown; pcs-parallel: parallel_for, map, reduce, scan, Scope; pcs-graph: TaskGraph::submit, TaskGraph::await_all; pcs-sync: Latch, Barrier, WaitGroup No breaking changes within MAJOR; ≥ 1 MINOR deprecation notice before removal
Beta pcs-sched: SchedPolicy variants; pcs-numa: public NUMA API; pcs-metrics: SchedMetrics field set May change in MINOR with deprecation warning
Internal pcs-deque: ChaseLevDeque, Stealer (re-exported, low-level); pcs-worker: Worker internals May change in MINOR; not intended for direct application use
Experimental pcs-trace: span event schema, binary ring-buffer format; flamegraph export API May change in PATCH; stabilizes at 1.0

All public enums are #[non_exhaustive] from initial publication to allow variant addition in MINOR.

Trace Format Compatibility

  • Binary trace format: schema_version: u8 in 8-byte header; readers skip unknown fields (forward compatibility).
  • New optional span fields: additive in MINOR; old readers ignore.
  • Field removal or type change: requires schema_version bump + MAJOR version.
  • JSON-ND export: field additions backward-compatible; field renames require MAJOR.
  • Flamegraph SVG: inferno-compatible; governed by pinned inferno crate version in Cargo.lock.
  • Conversion tool pcs-trace-migrate provided for MAJOR format upgrades; supports reading prior two MAJOR versions.

Metrics Format Compatibility

  • StealStats / SchedMetrics: additive counter fields in MINOR; existing counter semantics never changed without MAJOR.
  • Prometheus text format: label additions non-breaking in MINOR; label renames require MAJOR.
  • ThreadPool::metrics() return type: new fields added via ..Default::default() pattern; callers pattern-matching exhaustively must update.
  • Dashboard JSON (if exported by operator tooling): versioned separately; no stability guarantee before 1.0.

Storage / Persistence Compatibility

  • No persistent on-disk scheduler state between process lifetimes; no migration needed for in-process task state.
  • Trace files: binary format versioned (schema_version); pcs-trace-migrate converts between MAJOR versions; backward-compatible reads for ≥ 2 prior MAJOR schema versions.
  • Metrics snapshots (if written to disk by operator tooling): format versioned in file header; backward-compatible reads for ≥ 2 prior MINOR versions.
  • Flamegraph SVG files: no version header; format stable as long as inferno crate version pinned; re-generation from trace files is always possible.
  • Cargo.lock: checked into version control; provides reproducible builds; must be updated and reviewed on all dependency upgrades.

Wire / IPC Compatibility

  • pcs has no wire protocol; it is a library with no network or IPC surface.
  • Inter-process interaction: none. Each process holds its own ThreadPool; no shared state across process boundaries.
  • If future versions expose a metrics endpoint over HTTP: that endpoint's schema follows the Metrics Format Compatibility rules above; breaking changes require MAJOR.
  • If pcs-trace exposes a streaming socket (future): governed by the Trace Format Compatibility rules; schema_version in each frame header.

Type and Schema Evolution Rules

Type Allowed in MINOR Requires MAJOR
PoolConfig Add fields with Default impl; deprecate old fields Remove or rename fields; change field semantics
TaskId(u64) None (opaque) Change inner representation
TaskState (non_exhaustive) Add new variant Remove variant; change discriminant values
SchedPolicy (non_exhaustive) Add variant Remove variant; change default
StealStats / SchedMetrics Add atomic counter fields Remove fields; change field semantics
Scope<'s> Add methods Change lifetime variance; change blocking contract
Future<T> / Promise<T> Add methods Change wait() scheduling participation contract
GraphNode / Dependency Add optional metadata fields Change pending_deps AtomicUsize semantics
ChaseLevDeque<T> None (internal; changes allowed in MINOR) Public signature change affecting Stable tier callers

MSRV Policy

  • Minimum Supported Rust Version (MSRV) stated in rust-version field in workspace Cargo.toml.
  • MSRV bumped only in MINOR with one-release (one MINOR version) advance notice.
  • MSRV never bumped in PATCH.
  • Rationale: memory-ordering primitives, std::sync::atomic, and std::thread::available_parallelism are used; these stabilized in Rust 1.55–1.59; MSRV ≥ 1.65 (let-else stable) targeted.

Rolling-Upgrade Guarantees

  • Library upgrade within MINOR: full recompile required (Rust monomorphisation; no ABI stability guarantee).
  • Multiple ThreadPool instances mixing old + new version in the same process: not supported; upgrade requires process restart.
  • Inter-process compatibility (trace files, metrics exports): backward-compatible for 2 prior MINOR versions; MAJOR bumps require migration tool.
  • No wire protocol between scheduler instances (library, not service); no rolling-upgrade concern for scheduler-to-scheduler communication.

Deprecation Policy

  1. Mark symbol #[deprecated(since = "X.Y.0", note = "use Replacement instead")] in the MINOR that introduces the replacement.
  2. Deprecated symbol removed no sooner than the next MAJOR version.
  3. Deprecated metrics fields emit deprecated_metric_access counter increment and log a single WARN per pool lifetime.
  4. Deprecated PoolConfig fields: compile-time warning via #[deprecated]; adapter shim auto-populates replacement field.

Cross-Crate Dependency Contracts

Consumer Provider Contracted Interface Stability
pcs-worker pcs-deque ChaseLevDeque<Task>::push / pop / steal; Stealer<Task>::steal Internal; stable within MAJOR
pcs-pool pcs-worker Worker::run_loop; thread-local current-worker handle Internal; stable within MAJOR
pcs-parallel pcs-pool, pcs-worker ThreadPool::spawn, Scope::spawn, scope() Stable
pcs-graph pcs-pool, pcs-sync Latch; pending_deps AtomicUsize decrement-and-check contract Beta
pcs-metrics all StealStats aggregation; SchedMetrics read Beta
pcs-numa pcs-pool CpuSet, NumaNode, topology_detect() Beta
pcs-cli (benchmarks) pcs-parallel, pcs-pool Public stable API only; updated per MINOR if API evolves Experimental (bench binary, not lib)
pcs-sched pcs-worker, pcs-pool SchedPolicy variants consumed by victim selection and work-first/help-first logic Beta
pcs-sync pcs-worker, pcs-graph Latch::count_down / wait; Barrier::arrive_and_wait; WaitGroup Stable

Invariant Traceability

Compatibility guarantees are grounded in the system invariants. Changes that would violate an invariant require a MAJOR bump regardless of API surface impact.

Invariant Sections that may change it Required bump
I1 (single owner) ChaseLevDeque API, TaskState transitions MAJOR
I2 (wait-free owner, single-winner steal) pcs-deque internal, Stealer API MAJOR
I3 (no ABA, monotonic top) pcs-deque internal; top type widening PATCH (widening ok) / MAJOR (semantic change)
I4 (join returns only after a and b done) Scope, join API contract MAJOR
I5 (DAG ready iff all deps satisfied) GraphNode, pending_deps AtomicUsize MAJOR
I6 (pool not > num_threads workers) PoolConfig, compensation thread count MINOR (increase bound ok) / MAJOR (semantic change)
I7 (panic → Panicked, re-raise at scope) TaskState, Scope, panic propagation MAJOR
I8 (Scope blocks until all tasks terminal) Scope<'s>, Latch usage MAJOR
I9 (reduce == sequential for associative ops, FP documented) ParallelIterator reduce, scan MAJOR (change documented FP contract)
I10 (shutdown: all tasks terminal, all threads joined) ThreadPool::shutdown, drain logic MAJOR

Testing & Verification Strategy

Unit Testing

Layer What How
pcs-deque push/pop single-owner correctness deterministic sequences; verify LIFO order; assert no element lost
pcs-deque steal FIFO ordering two-thread harness: owner pushes N, thief steals; verify FIFO
pcs-deque ABA safety (monotonic top) force wrap-around at u32::MAX with u64 counter; verify no double-steal
pcs-deque grow/realloc under owner push until buffer doubles; verify all elements survive
pcs-worker local pop path unit-test worker loop iteration without spawning threads
pcs-core TaskState transitions assert only valid transitions: Pending→Ready→Running→Done/Panicked
pcs-graph pending_deps decrement construct chain A→B→C; resolve A; verify B becomes Ready; resolve B; verify C
pcs-graph cycle rejection introduce back-edge; assert Err on submit
pcs-sync Latch count-down count = N; N threads count-down; waiter unblocks exactly once
pcs-sync Future::wait return value promise fulfilled before wait; promise fulfilled concurrently; both paths
pcs-parallel split-at semantics split Producer at midpoint; both halves span correct index ranges
pcs-numa domain assignment mock topology; verify worker placed in correct domain per cpu-id

Integration Testing

Test Scope Assertion
fork-join merge sort pcs-parallel + pcs-pool result equals sequential sort; for N in {0,1,1024,65536}
DAG linear chain pcs-graph + pcs-pool all nodes execute in topological order; no node runs before deps
DAG diamond pcs-graph + pcs-pool both parallel arms run; join node runs after both arms
scope lifetime pcs-parallel Scope scope returns only after all spawned tasks reach Done; I8
panic propagation pcs-parallel + pcs-pool child panics; join re-raises with original message; I7
multi-scope nesting pcs-parallel nested scopes; inner completes before outer; no task leaks
cancellation drain pcs-worker + pcs-pool cancel token set; pool drains without executing Pending tasks
shutdown ordering pcs-pool shutdown() joins all threads; no task remains Running after return; I10
injector overflow pcs-pool external threads submit >N tasks; all complete; no drop
parallel_for correctness pcs-parallel fill array via parallel_for; result equals sequential fill
parallel reduce pcs-parallel sum 1..N; result == N*(N+1)/2; I9
parallel scan pcs-parallel prefix sum; verify each element against sequential prefix

Differential Testing (Reference Implementation)

  • Reference: single-threaded sequential execution of the same task closure / iterator body.
  • Harness: pcs-cli differential_runner accepts a workload closure, runs it both ways, compares outputs.
  • Inputs: randomized array contents, random task counts (property-based via proptest).
  • Assertions: bitwise equality for integer reductions; epsilon tolerance documented for float reassociation (I9).
  • DAG differential: execute graph on pool; replay topological sort sequentially; compare per-node result.

Property-Based Testing

Property Generator Library
deque never loses elements random push/pop/steal sequence, arbitrary interleaving proptest
no double-execution (I1) concurrent push + steal; log executions proptest + std::sync::atomic log
parallel_reduce == sequential fold for associative ops random Vec<u64>, random op from {+, *, min, max} proptest
Scope: all spawned tasks complete (I8) random tree depth/fan-out proptest
DAG: no task runs before all deps done (I5) random DAG topology, random latencies proptest + ordering log
TaskState machine: no invalid transitions random event stream per task proptest

Fuzzing

Target Fuzzer What varies
ChaseLevDeque concurrent ops cargo-fuzz + libfuzzer byte stream decoded as op sequence (push/pop/steal)
TaskGraph::submit cargo-fuzz adjacency list bytes; exercise cycle detection, edge cases
PoolConfig deserialization cargo-fuzz config bytes; assert no panic on malformed input
Scope spawn sequences cargo-fuzz spawn count, task depth; verify termination + no leaks
  • Fuzz corpora committed to fuzz/corpus/; CI runs each target for 60 s per merge.
  • Sanitizers: AddressSanitizer + ThreadSanitizer on fuzz builds.

Deterministic-Replay / Determinism Testing (Phase 14)

  • Single-thread mode: PoolConfig { num_threads: 1, policy: WorkFirst } + seeded RNG disables stealing randomness.
  • Replay harness: record task execution order (trace IDs) on run 1; assert identical order on run 2 with same seed.
  • Use-case: reproducing race conditions; regression test suite runs workloads in single-thread mode to detect logic bugs independent of schedule.
  • loom model-checking (see Phase 14): loom drives ChaseLevDeque under all valid interleavings; asserts I1, I2, I3.

Chaos / Fault Injection

Fault Injection Point Assertion
Random worker panic inject panic! in task body at random pool continues; other tasks complete; I7 propagates to scope
Worker thread kill (OS signal simulation) replacement thread injected by pool no task permanently lost; pool recovers
Steal always fails (CAS sabotage in test build) #[cfg(test)] steal_fail_rate flag workers park; injector unblocks them; no deadlock
NUMA migration mid-task affinity change via test hook task completes on wrong domain; no correctness failure; metric logged
Backpressure saturation inject submitters faster than workers drain submit() applies backpressure (P10); memory stays bounded

Soak / Long-Running Tests

  • 30-min soak: parallel mergesort on 64 M elements, 8 threads; monitor RSS (no unbounded growth), steal counts stable.
  • 8-hour stress: DAG with 10 k nodes per batch, continuous submission; assert no memory leak via periodic jemalloc epoch stats.
  • Idle soak: pool with zero work for 60 s; assert all workers parked; CPU usage < 0.1%.

Performance Regression

  • criterion benches gated in CI (Phase 14); baseline committed as JSON.
  • Regression threshold: >5% slowdown on parallel_mergesort_8t or steal_throughput triggers CI failure.
  • Separate perf-regression job runs nightly on pinned hardware; results posted to perf/ branch.

Conformance Validation

  • Conformance suite: pcs-cli conformance runs all integration fixtures and asserts I1–I10 invariants explicitly.
  • Each invariant has a dedicated test file tests/invariant_iN.rs.
  • CI gate: conformance suite must pass at 0 failures before any merge.
